# Raphael product rules

Raphael is built so that everyone can reach AI, not only people who already understand models, providers and keys. A small frustration at the start is enough to make someone give up on the app for good, so every flow is designed for a first-time user who has none of our accounts: the app works out what it can by itself, asks one clear thing at a time, says what each choice means and what it costs, never defaults to one particular service, and never leaves a dead end. A screen that makes the user think about how Raphael is built, or stop to wonder what to do next, is a defect like any other.

## The Keychain is asked once, at need, and a Deny stands

On a Mac the owner was asked for the Keychain at every message, could not send anything while the question stood, and Deny
only brought the question back. The macOS Keychain asks before a program reads the secret of an item that another build
wrote, and its Allow answers that one read; the core read every key again for each turn, list and status. Apple's own rule
is to ask when a feature needs it, once, and to respect a refusal with a way back.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

The watch first makes a listen-only tap of the keys alone (`computer/watch.rs` `keys_delivered`) and starts only when it is made; otherwise the run is refused (`watch`) and the core says `computer_watch`. | cargo | hard | app/src-tauri/src/computer/watch.rs::a_run_is_watched_only_once_the_keys_alone_are_delivered; features/conversation.md (the watch on the owner's input) |

A lookup the Keychain cannot answer (a locked keychain, a status it does not explain, the lookup itself failing) never breaks a status or a list: `peek` and `filled` then count the secret as kept but unread and the read when it is used says what is there, while `has`, asked to prove a secret gone, raises; a delete the Keychain answers with errSecItemNotFound is gone with no lookup, and one with any other answer is checked and raises if the secret is still there, so deleting a member whose channels were never connected never depends on the lookup and a member whose secrets each deleted without an error is deleted even when the lookup after cannot answer; a secret is read only when it is used, at most once in a run, and reads wait for one another so an item is never asked about twice at once; the owner's Deny (errSecUserCanceled or errSecAuthFailed) stands for the run: nothing more is asked and every read is refused at once (`keychain.Refused`) until the owner presses 再問一次 or Raphael starts again. ## A member is shared by one link

A member template can be saved as a file or shared by a link that opens a preview. Link sharing uses the owner's share service
(share-template/), trusted the way the store trusts the owner's catalogue.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SHARE-01 | 2026-11-18 | A template card shares its recipe by a link only when the owner's share service is set: `SHARE_URL` of core/src/raphael_core/members/share_link.py, an HTTPS address (share-template/README.md holds the owner's steps; `RAPHAEL_SHARE_URL` stands in for a developer's own). Then the card's primary button is 複製連結: the core sends the scanned recipe (a credential still refuses it), keeps the link and the key that takes it down, and the page copies the link and says so; a later press copies the same link and sends nothing. A shared card shows its link and 停止分享, which takes the link down at the service before the card forgets it, so the card can be shared again with a new link. The key never reaches the page. A service that cannot be reached, or answers anything but a well-formed link of its own address, leaves the card unshared and the owner is told in their words. With no share service, the card offers primary 儲存檔案 and 查看內容, including cards stored in the legacy published state. The card offers no GitHub actions. The service keeps the recipe and only the SHA-256 of the key, shows whoever opens the link a preview page with no scripts and every value escaped, and gives the recipe as a .raphael-member file that opens in Raphael's import review. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/test_chat_setup_share.py::test_without_a_share_service_no_card_offers_a_link_and_nothing_is_sent; core/tests/test_chat_setup_share.py::test_a_shared_card_keeps_one_link_until_the_owner_takes_it_down; core/tests/test_chat_setup_share.py::test_a_share_service_that_answers_anything_else_leaves_the_card_unshared; core/tests/test_chat_setup_share.py::test_an_unreachable_share_service_and_a_plain_address_are_refused; core/tests/test_chat_setup_share.py::test_secret_findings_block_snapshot_export_and_publish_even_after_live_member_is_clean; app/src/main/chat-cards.test.ts::with a share service the card copies a link first, and a key inside stops it; app/src/main/chat-cards.test.ts::sharing copies the link the core made, and a failure copies nothing and says so; app/src/main/chat-cards.test.ts::a shared card shows its link, copies it again without asking the core, and stops sharing; app/src/main/chat-cards.test.ts::a legacy published template offers the same file and review actions as a draft; app/scripts/share-worker.test.mjs; member-template-published; member-template-shared |

## Every app wears its own mark, at one size

The owner's 工具 tab of a member showed the plug for 「Google」 and Outlook visibly larger than Notion, GitHub and the other marks of the list. Causes: the Google account had no mark of its own (other places borrowed Gmail's, the 工具 tab fell to the plug that stands for any unknown app), and the marks' box (`.mono`, 28 x 22) let a picture's percentage height go unresolved: an inline mark was drawn 22 px while a catalogue picture with its own 128 px size took the box's 28 px width. Now there is one mark for the Google account, one generic mark for every app nobody drew, and one square for every mark, and a rule test fails on an app without a mark, on the plug standing for an app and on a stylesheet that draws a mark wider than tall.

## An address in words read whole is a link

An all-day item a member had synced from another app into the calendar held a page's address in its notes, and the detail card drew it as dead text: only the words of a message were links. Now one helper draws plain words with their addresses as links wherever words are read whole, and one listener opens every link of the window.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-LINK-01 | 2026-11-16 | Every http or https address in words the window shows whole (the notes of the calendar's detail card, a conversation card's words and a question card's title, a routine's instructions, a sent mail and the message a permission card would send, a connection's technical reason, a room's decisions, a template's words, a connected step's result, the release notes and a connected app's description) is a link made by the one helper `linkedText` (message-content.ts, which shares `bareUrl` with the message renderer): every character is escaped first, the link's words are the address itself as written with no tooltip, and it ends at a space, a quote, CJK punctuation, a full stop, comma or bracket that closes the sentence, and at Chinese, Japanese or Korean words set right after it unless they start a part of the address (`/`, `=`, `?`, `#`, `&` or `@` before them); `javascript:`, `data:`, `file:`, bare `www.` and an address with a sign-in in it stay words. A press, a Ctrl or Shift press and a middle press open it in the owner's browser through the one capture listener `installLinkOpener` (link-opener.ts, started in main.ts), never the window and never the row, card or panel around it, with the shell's `open_external_url` the only gate; a right press keeps the app's own menu. Words in a field being edited, a one-line preview cut short and words inside a button stay plain. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::a press opens the address once in the browser and never the row around it, which a press beside the link still opens; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::Ctrl, Shift and a middle click open it the same way, and a right click is left to the app's menu; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::the calendar's detail card links the address in its notes, opens it once and stays open; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::Chinese, Japanese or Korean words right after an address end it, unless they start a part of the address; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::javascript:, data:, file:, a bare www. and an address with a sign-in in it are never links; app/src/main/text-links.test.ts::text that is edited stays text: the calendar's form keeps the address in its field |

## A member's own background work is never queued for ever

Two members showed as working for hours: each conversation turn that handed work to its own member waited (`waiting`) for a child that stayed `queued`, with no model call, through two restarts. Cause: the core builds the background service before its event loop runs (`__main__.main` builds the app, then `run_stdio` starts the loop), and `use_team` puts back what the last run left queued (a waiting parent whose child ended while the app was down). That start-up pump carried the task out with `asyncio.ensure_future` while no loop was running, so the task was parked on a loop that never runs: it never started or ended, yet it stayed in `_active` and held its member's place and one of the three `MAX_PARALLEL` places for ever, so every later task of that member (and a cancelled parent's place too) queued behind it. A waiting turn never held a place (the three-waiting-turns candidate was tested and is not a cause).

The composer's right-hand button is Stop while `composerTask` (`pages/agent.ts`) finds, in the loaded turns of the open conversation, a turn that is `running` or `confirm`, or whose task is `queued`, `running` or `waiting`, or a request sent and not yet written (`outgoing`), or a voice message being transcribed (`hearing`, at most `HEARING_MAX_MS`); a recording shows the send glyph, not Stop. A group room's composer is Stop while its exchange is `queued`/`running` or a hand-off of the room is `queued`/`running`/`waiting` (`group-chats.ts`, `room-thread.ts`), and reads again on `CoreReady` already. It can outlive the work in three ways: the core leaves a task `queued` or a parent `waiting` for ever (the cause above); the owner's Stop cannot end it (`worker.cancel` of a task that is `queued` but is in neither the queue nor any carrier answered `cancelled: false` and changed nothing); or the page never reads the end (a restarted core expires the turns and interrupts the tasks and sends only `CoreReady`, which the conversation page ignored, and an event that never arrives was never made up for).

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PUMP-01 | 2026-11-15 | The queue is only ever worked from the running loop: while no loop runs (start-up, `use_team`) `_ensure_pump` starts nothing and leaves every queued task queued, and `BackgroundService.start` (called by `core.hello`, once the loop is there) takes the queue up, so no task is ever parked on a loop that never runs or holds a member's place without running. A task that waits for its children (an asker turn, or a worker that handed work on and parked) holds no place a child needs. A hand-off that never began and was queued more than `STALE_QUEUE_MS` (2 hours) before the app next starts is not started unseen: it ends `interrupted` with reason `stale_queue`, its report and its turn closed as for any task a restart ends, and its waiting parent is woken to answer with that result; a younger one is put back as before. The owner's own stuck rows leave that way at the next start: the children end `stale_queue`, the parents answer once, and nothing stays queued. Start-up leaves the turns that kept work still holds (a queued hand-off's work turn and the colleague's brief it answers, a waiting task's turn) to that work: the agent service's sweep of turns left running does not expire them. A room drive asked for before the loop runs is left to the rooms' start. A child the restart cut off (running, or waiting for a yes) ends `interrupted` and wakes its waiting parent once. A member could not end its own earlier task, and the owner could not stop the work behind a conversation from the interface; the thread after a stop was to stay clean and tidy. Cause: the background tools a member had (`handoff`, `work_status`) could start work and read a room's board but never end a task, and every cancel (`_conclude` with `cancelled`) wrote 「任務已中止。已執行 N 次工具呼叫。」 into the turn, the report and the overlay, and a `stale_queue` ending wrote an interrupted report.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-STOP-01 | 2026-11-15 | A stop somebody asked for says nothing: when the owner stops work (the composer's Stop, `worker.cancel`, a session's stop), when a member ends its own task, or when a parent's end takes its children with it, the task ends `cancelled` with no report text, no line and no count of calls in the conversation (the turn keeps the words it already had and is closed `cancelled`), no `worker.report` on the overlay, and a task stopped before it did anything leaves no line in the thread at all (a stop after some work keeps the steps it made). The same holds for a `stale_queue` ending: no report, `announced` already 1, its turn closed `cancelled` and empty, its waiting parent woken. An unexpected ending (a crash, a restart that lost a running task, the worker lost) keeps its one short report line. A conversation turn that ends without its answer (stopped, failed, refused) ends the pieces it handed on that wait (for the owner's yes or answer, or for their own colleagues) as Stop does, their card or question closed, silently; a later yes finds it `cancelled` and makes no call. | pytest, vitest | hard | core/tests/background/test_service.py::test_a_task_the_user_stops_ends_at_once_with_what_was_done; core/tests/background/test_endings.py::test_waiting_tree_cancel_keeps_reply_counts_and_cancelled_status; core/tests/background/test_queue_pump.py::test_a_hand_off_that_waited_for_hours_ends_with_its_reason_and_wakes_its_parent; core/tests/background/test_stop_work.py::test_a_member_lists_its_own_tasks_and_stops_one_without_a_word; core/tests/test_e2e_worker.py::test_a_task_the_user_stops_ends_quietly_and_the_worker_stays_for_the_next; app/src/main/conversation.test.ts::a task that was stopped before it did anything leaves no line, and one stopped after some work keeps its steps (R-STOP-01); core/tests/background/test_stop_states.py::test_a_turn_that_ends_without_its_answer_ends_the_work_it_handed_on_that_waits_for_the_owner; features/work-truth.md |

## A conversation read at its bottom stays at its bottom

When a member replied, the thread jumped up a little from its bottom. Cause: following the bottom was a correction queued by the thread's own draws, so a height change no draw made was not followed: a picture the core read back late (a member's picture, the large pictures of a conversation just opened), and the same picture standing empty for a frame each time its line was drawn again, left the thread a few to some hundreds of pixels above its bottom, and a growth past 80 px also made it stop following.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-FOLLOW-01 | 2026-11-14 | A conversation the owner reads at its bottom (private, Raphael's or a room's) stays at its bottom in every painted frame, whatever changes its height or its scroller's: their message, the work line and the slime, the words, pictures and cards that come late, the end of the work, paced bubbles, reactions, the composer and what stands under the thread, and a conversation that opens with pictures still on their way. One place keeps it there (message-rich.ts `readingAnchor`): a ResizeObserver on the scroller and its content writes the bottom in the frame of the change, while the thread follows. A picture drawn again when its line changes takes the copy already loaded at once and never stands empty. The owner's own scrolling up (the wheel, also over the header, the scroll keys, a touch) lets go of the bottom before it moves anything; from then on nothing moves the line they read, however near the bottom, until they are back at the very bottom or press the latest button; a press on the floating row of a waiting question (R-OWNER-03) is their own move to that question, in a room as in a member's conversation. No timer and no smooth scrolling hide a jump. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a thread at its bottom stays there through every change of its height or its scroller's, in the frame of the change; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a thread the owner scrolled up stays where they read, near the bottom or far from it, until they are back at its bottom; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a scroll still going up does not take the bottom again, a thread that cannot scroll is not let go, and the latest button follows; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::a picture drawn again takes the copy already loaded at once and never stands empty; app/scripts/look/follow-probe.test.mjs::fails a picture already drawn that stands shorter again, while a picture still on its way only grows; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a thread the owner scrolled up that comes to its bottom by getting shorter follows again; app/src/main/conversation-scroll.test.ts::the thread hears a wheel turned over the header before it moves, so a wheel up there lets go of the bottom too (R-FOLLOW-01); follow-reply-near; follow-reply-bottom; follow-reply-reading; follow-open-pictures; owner-question-brought; owner-question-pending-room |
| R-FOLLOW-02 | 2026-11-18 | A conversation read in the middle keeps the line read when its column changes width: the details panel dragged, opened or closed, the window resized. A wider thread is shorter, and the place read can lie past its new end: the browser then clamps the view to the bottom with a scroll that may come before the width is reported (closing the details panel jumped to the newest message). That scroll is the width change's, not the owner's: the place read before it is put back (reading-place.ts) and stays theirs (`conversation-hold` to message-rich.ts `readingAnchor`), so the thread does not start following the newest turn; a thread read at its bottom stays at its bottom. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/reading-place.test.ts::a wider, shorter thread clamped to its new end before the width is reported keeps the place read in the middle, and says the owner holds it; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::a place in the middle that a width change put back is the owner's: the end in view for a moment does not make the thread follow; panel-close-keeps-place; panel-drag-keeps-place |
| R-FOLLOW-03 | 2026-11-18 | On a Mac, a conversation kept at its bottom is painted afresh after it grows: WebKit left the whole thread black, under a normal header and composer, when a member's reply landed (the longer, the surer: a long one all black) or its work ended, until a scroll. Whenever the thread's one stick to the bottom (message-rich.ts `readingAnchor`) takes the new end, the view is repainted once the following settles, and at least every 600 ms while a reply keeps coming, in a member's conversation and in a room. The repaint is the one width changes use (reading-place.ts `repaintView`): one nudge up and back in two frames, never ending a held place, a view a few px short of the bottom left alone, a scroll of the owner's own in that frame kept. The stick itself reads no geometry; Windows scrolls nothing. | vitest | hard | app/src/main/message-rich-mac.test.ts::a long reply that lands in a following thread is painted afresh on a Mac once it settles, and at least every few hundred ms while it keeps coming; app/src/main/message-rich.test.ts::the stick to the bottom writes a clamped position and reads no container geometry |

Twelve pictures in the composer ran past its right edge, out of reach of the mouse, and once sent they stood as a two-column grid of large tiles (about 230 px each) several screens tall. The composer's row now scrolls with the mouse, and sent pictures stand side by side as small squares.

It is the shared chip row (`chip-scroll.ts`): no scrollbar, an arrow and a fade only at an end with more behind it, a picture reached by Tab brought into view, the × of every picture and the composer's plus unchanged. ## Long work tells the owner how it goes

A member said nothing of its progress: a hand-off task of a member on Claude Code ran 37 minutes with 71 tool calls and wrote 142 steps and not one message until the owner stopped it, so the owner could not tell whether it had crashed. Cause: what a member says while it works is progress in the folded work (`_release_held`, only one opening is a bubble), and the API rules keep `messages` for an important result; nothing ever asked a working member for a word.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PROGRESS-01 | 2026-11-14 | A run the owner reads as it works (a turn of the owner's own conversation and a task in a member's private conversation, on API and CLI brains) keeps the time of its last bubble (`agent/progress_updates.py`; the opening counts, a progress line does not). When a tool result comes back 120 s into a run with no bubble, or 300 s after the last, it carries one line for the model (FIRST_UPDATE_S and NEXT_UPDATE_S, in one place): it has been N minutes since the owner was told anything, so before the next step write a two-sentence update (what is done, what is next) in plain words with no tool names; an API conversation turn is told to put it in `messages`, which NARRATION_RULES now allow for an update a reminder asks for. The line goes after the result and outside its untrusted wrapper (a CLI's MCP result, `ToolOutcome.note` in the worker loop, `host.tool`'s `note` for a background API task); stored tool events, CLI receipts and the CLI's tool conversation keep the real result. One reminder until the next bubble, said again only after another 300 s. While the run is past that time, what it says next is a bubble and not progress: a CLI's held or plain speech between steps (foreground, and a task's through its `_reply`), a background API task's words with its calls. Unchanged: the opening, the final answer, the folded work, routines (the owner reads only the last message), a meeting's reply (its words are its post), consultations, a colleague's question, greetings and memory passes, which keep no clock. The default prompt stays inside its budget (the default prompt test: before 16024 / 15202 tokens for an API / CLI brain, after 16023 / 15202, limits 16048 / 15212). | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_the_first_word_is_owed_after_two_minutes_and_the_next_five_minutes_after_the_last; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_run_that_told_the_owner_something_lately_is_asked_for_nothing; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_turn_is_asked_for_a_word_at_two_minutes_then_five_and_its_update_is_a_message; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_reminder_nobody_answered_comes_again_only_after_five_minutes; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_turn_whose_opening_was_shown_lately_is_asked_for_nothing_yet; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_conversation_turn_gets_the_reminder_and_shows_its_update; core/tests/agent/test_progress_updates.py::test_an_api_conversation_turn_is_asked_for_an_update_in_messages; core/tests/background/test_progress_updates.py::test_an_api_task_is_asked_for_a_word_and_its_update_reaches_the_owner; core/tests/background/test_progress_updates.py::test_a_cli_task_is_asked_for_a_word_and_its_update_reaches_reply; core/tests/members/test_management_discovery.py::test_default_production_prompt_stays_below_the_measured_pre_unit_budget |

## Our own media players, and media that load once

The app shows its own media (pictures, pages, HTML, video) with its own players, not the system's defaults, and caches whatever can be cached, so that opening a conversation does not load everything again. The inventory of every kind and how each is shown is in features/conversation.md (Our own media players).

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-MEDIA-01 | 2026-11-15 | Every stored video and sound in the app (a member's media message, the owner's own file, a room's, an older URL card, the full screen) plays in our own player (`media-player.ts`), never the browser's: the element has no `controls`, carries `controlslist="nodownload nofullscreen noremoteplayback noplaybackrate"`, `disablepictureinpicture` and `disableremoteplayback`, and a right click opens no native menu (the app's own right click, context-menu.ts). A video plays and pauses on a press on its picture, on Space or K anywhere in the player but on another button (Space presses that button), and on its play button. At rest it shows its first frame, a round play mark and its length; pointed at, focused or paused part-way it shows one bar on the photo scrim (the same in both themes): play, a scrubber that shows what was played and what is loaded, elapsed / total time, mute with its volume opening above it, the speed (a framed chip; 0.5× to 2× from the app's menu, which opens below the player so it never lies over the player's own words, the current one checked; in the app's full screen, where the player is the window, it opens above the bar instead, every speed on screen) and full screen. While it plays the bar leaves 2.5 s after the pointer last moved over it (not while the keyboard is in it or the speed menu is open). The arrows move 5 s, M mutes, F opens full screen. Full screen is the app's own: the lightbox, edge to edge under the caption strip at every size with its controls a size up, with the same video where it was (playing or not, its speed), left by its own button in its bar, Escape or a press outside (the lightbox's × is not drawn over a video), which gives the time back to the player in the thread (it plays on if it played). A small video keeps play, the scrubber and full screen (the time goes first, then sound and speed). A sound has the same family in one compact row: play, its name over the scrubber and its time, mute with its volume, speed. One player plays at a time. Every word is in every locale. Saving stays where it was: the owner's own file keeps its open button, a member's file is opened from the Library. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::no page draws the browser's own media player; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::a video and a sound draw none of the browser's controls: no download, picture-in-picture, casting or full screen of its own; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::a press on the picture, Space and the button play and pause a video; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::the scrubber shows what was played and what is loaded, its time, and moves the video; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::sound is muted from its button and M, with the volume beside it; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::one player plays at a time: another that starts pauses it; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::the speed is chosen from the app's menu, the current one checked; app/src/main/media-player.test.ts::full screen is the lightbox with the same video where it was, and leaving it gives the time back; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::our sound row plays, pauses, seeks and follows its duration and playback; app/src/main/attachment-layout.test.ts::pictures and video have no visible filename, size, or separate information card; app/scripts/look/audit.test.mjs::reports the browser's own media player as a native control, and our player as none (R-MEDIA-01); features/conversation.md (video-message, video-message-hover, video-message-playing, video-message-space, video-full-screen, video-lightbox-playing, audio-message, audio-speed-menu, video-full-screen-speed, generated-video) |

| R-MEDIA-03 | 2026-11-15 | Cause of the loading on every open: each picture of a conversation crossed IPC as base64 (`attachments.get`, decoded with `atob` into a Blob) every time the conversation was opened, because the thread's `reset()` revoked every Blob URL when it was left; videos and sounds went through Tauri's asset protocol, which sends no cache headers and names a disk path. Now a stored picture, video or sound comes by its opaque id from the shell's own protocol (`media_protocol.rs`; `http://raphael-media.localhost/<id>` on Windows): `attachment_source` asks the core (`attachments.path`, the same reviewed boundary, now with the stored type) and grants that one file to the main window; the protocol answers only the main window, only an id granted in this run, only raster pictures, video and audio, with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, `ETag: "<id>"`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, 304 for a matching If-None-Match and byte ranges of at most 2 MiB. A stored id names its bytes for good (written once under a fresh id, never rewritten), so the URL carries no per-session token: the grant is checked on the shell's side in each run and the cache key stays the same from one run to the next. The window keeps each id's URL for the session (`media-cache.ts`: asked once, whoever asks, at the same time or later) and holds up to 120 drawn pictures (192 MB decoded), so a conversation opened again sets its pictures in the same frame and asks the shell for nothing; an id the protocol does not serve falls back to `attachments.get` once, held as a Blob of bounded size. Measured in look (`media-reopen`, two pictures of about 1.7 MB, both themes at 1120, 720 and 1920): before, every open read both as 4,494,936 base64 characters over IPC, and the reopen painted in 389 to 460 ms on a quiet machine and 700 to 844 ms on a busier one; after, measured right after the busier run, the first open makes 2 grants and 2 protocol requests (3.37 MB) and the reopen no call and no request, painted in 110 to 237 ms. Nothing that changes (a page being edited, a computer's frame) uses this cache. | vitest, look, pytest, cargo test | hard | app/src/main/media-cache.test.ts::an id's URL is asked of the shell once a session, whoever asks and however often; app/src/main/media-cache.test.ts::a failed ask is not remembered: the next one asks again; app/src/main/media-cache.test.ts::pictures kept decoded are bounded by count and by decoded size, the oldest going first; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::a stored picture comes by its id from the media protocol, once a session, and a thread opened again draws it at once; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::two threads that ask for the same picture at the same time ask the shell once; app/src/main/attachment-media.test.ts::a stored copy the protocol does not serve is read once as a Blob instead; app/src/csp.test.ts::stored attachments load from the app's own media protocol as pictures and media only, never as scripts, frames or connections; core/tests/services/test_attachment_upload.py::test_the_path_of_a_stored_file_names_the_type_it_was_stored_as; app/src-tauri/src/media_protocol.rs (cargo test: a_whole_file_comes_with_the_cache_headers_and_exact_bytes, a_copy_the_browser_holds_is_not_sent_again, one_response_carries_at_most_two_mebibytes, what_was_not_granted_or_is_not_an_id_is_not_found, only_pictures_video_and_sound_are_granted); app/src-tauri/src/commands.rs (cargo test: an_attachment_is_granted_to_the_media_protocol_not_the_asset_protocol); features/conversation.md (media-reopen, generated-image, picture-message) |

## A member reads, searches and changes the files of its own computer

Without file tools a member would read, search and change the files of its own computer through `vm_exec` (`cat`, `sed`, heredocs): the head of the output only, no line numbers, no paging, no check that the text it replaced was the text it had read, and whole files under the 16,000-character argument cap. These rows give it five file tools on its own computer: `read_file`, `edit_file`, `write_file`, `glob` and `grep`. They are computer tools (server `computer`, listed whole, T0 in the sandbox), so they exist exactly when the member has a computer and reach every brain, CLI brains through the computer MCP server; the CLI vendors' own file and shell tools stay off (they would act on the owner's PC outside Raphael's card, R-PC-02). No approval card is added for a member's own files.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

On each computer tool that reaches the driver or acts outward it is one sentence after the tool's own line (`VM_CONTRACT`, 166 characters: outward acts need the owner's yes for the exact act first, approve on CLI, sandbox success is not approval), so a tool's description stays about the tool and its policy stays in the system prompt. It was 350 characters on 34 listed tools: a member with a computer now carries about 1,560 fewer tokens in every request (measured with `estimate_tokens` over the listed computer definitions: 18,144 to 16,580 at a seat, 17,986 to 16,422 per member). With the five file tools below added it is 17,693 at a seat and 17,535 per member, still about 450 fewer than before them; the computer catalog's descriptions (as `test_catalog_stays` sums them) went from 18,364 at a seat to 11,740, and are 14,054 with the file tools, under a ceiling lowered from 19,500 to 14,500. ## A failure is information: a member keeps going until no plausible way is left

Members gave up too fast. A member treats a failed call as information, takes the next plausible way, and asks the owner only for what only the owner can give, or when the ways left cost far more than the result is worth. Cause, from a language-teaching member's turn (told to use its own computer for a news page, it read the lead, had its page refused twice, ended by declining to work around the block and never opened its browser): every word of the harness on that path said stop. These rows change the harness for every member and every brain; no member's own routines, skills, instructions or memory are edited.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PERSIST-01 | 2026-11-14 | No prompt caps persistence. Gone: "Diagnosis: inspect once, change approach once, then report the exact blocker" (the about index every prompt reads), "change approach at most once before reporting the blocker" (the shared doctrine), "use a different approach at most once" (the corrective note), "Do not try another command or path" (the data floor's refusal), "report it plainly after one check" (the guardrails' note) and the worker's own "When a tool fails, try another way" (said once now, for all). One rule stands in their place, in the shared contract that every conversation (API, narrated, CLI; chief and members) and every task reads: a failed call is information; read its cause, fix what you can, then take the next plausible way (another tool, other arguments, another source, your computer's browser when you have one); ask the owner only for what only they can give, or when the ways left cost far more than the result is worth; never claim that a failed call succeeded. A part answer looks for the rest first, then names a gap left once (R-WORK-17). The default prompt stays inside its budget (the default prompt test: before 15939 / 15204 tokens for an API / CLI brain, after 15929 / 15206, limits 16048 / 15212). | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_persistence.py::test_every_prompt_carries_the_one_rule_and_none_of_the_caps; core/tests/agent/test_persistence.py::test_the_rule_is_said_once_in_the_shared_contract_and_the_diagnosis_cap_is_gone; core/tests/agent/test_persistence.py::test_a_part_answer_looks_for_the_rest_first; core/tests/agent/test_persistence.py::test_the_data_floor_refusal_points_at_the_right_tools_and_no_longer_forbids_every_other_way; core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_a_gap_is_named_once_and_only_when_it_matters_for_every_speaker; core/tests/members/test_management_discovery.py::test_default_production_prompt_stays_below_the_measured_pre_unit_budget |

## The interface language is the window's alone

The interface language selects interface text only: it is told to no model and decides no reply language. This replaces the configured reply language every external turn and every background task was told (`reply_context`, 「Owner's configured reply language: …」) and the interface locale every system prompt named (`interface_context`, 「Interface locale: … This selects fixed UI labels only.」), and supersedes R-AG-LANG-01's "interface metadata" in features/conversation.md.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

With the interface in Japanese and the owner writing Traditional Chinese, everything a model is sent is the same as on any other interface. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_conversation_language.py::test_no_prompt_builder_takes_a_language; core/tests/agent/test_conversation_language.py::test_the_interface_language_has_no_words_for_a_model_left; core/tests/agent/test_conversation_language.py::test_the_reply_kinds_are_one_fixed_set_and_read_as_the_windows_prefix; core/tests/agent/test_conversation_language.py::test_a_reply_to_chinese_words_on_a_japanese_interface_names_no_language; core/tests/members/test_interface_language.py::test_a_member_is_told_nothing_of_a_japanese_interface_when_the_owner_writes_chinese; core/tests/members/test_interface_language.py::test_a_messaging_chat_on_a_japanese_interface_is_not_told_to_reply_in_japanese; core/tests/background/test_interface_language.py::test_a_routine_written_in_chinese_is_told_nothing_of_a_japanese_interface; core/tests/agent/test_owner_chat_language.py::test_an_external_turn_is_told_no_interface_language; core/tests/agent/test_ui_languages.py::test_every_locale_has_fixed_copy_and_no_brain_path_names_it; core/tests/computer/test_run.py::test_the_prompt_asks_for_the_owners_language_and_names_no_interface_language; core/tests/agent/test_cli_confirmation.py::test_an_expired_confirmation_declines_only_its_call; core/tests/agent/test_cli_confirmation.py::test_an_expired_claude_permission_names_the_unanswered_action |

## A member does what its owner asks, in full, and says plainly what it did not do

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-WORK-16 | 2026-11-14 | A language-teaching member, asked for daily reading pages from real news for the owner's own study with the source credited, lectured on copyright in five replies and then delivered a page of only a headline and one sentence, though the owner had said it was for their own use and asked for everything written in full at their level with the source's link. Cause: nothing in Raphael's prompts speaks of copyright, so the refusal was the model's own; what the prompts lacked was that this is the owner's own private app and their own use, and what they had pushed the other way (a closed page was to be replaced by "another source … rewritten simply for a learner"). A member's prompt (reply, CLI conversation, task, routine; the chief's task too) now says: what the owner asks for their own use is made in full, from the source read: the complete rewrite at their level, every vocabulary and grammar note, the translations, the source's link inside the page; a headline, a lead or a summary is never the delivery; once they have said what it is for, no refusal, lecture or repeated reservation; if the model's own policy really stops it copying a text word for word, it says so once in one short sentence and delivers the complete rewritten material anyway. The chief's conversation prompt keeps its size budget (the default prompt test, 15909 of 16048 tokens for an API brain and 15174 of 15212 for a CLI one) and does not carry the block. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_every_member_prompt_says_to_do_what_the_owner_asks_in_full_in_their_own_app; core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_the_chiefs_conversation_prompt_is_not_given_the_members_block; core/tests/members/test_management_discovery.py::test_default_production_prompt_stays_below_the_measured_pre_unit_budget |

Cause in the prompts: a report was to include "checks and anything unfinished" (task, routine and CLI reports), a part-answer was to "name the gap" with no limit, and a closed page was a gap to name. Honesty stays (never invent a fact; only claim what a tool result established), the self-protective boilerplate goes: what was not done is said once, plainly, at the end, and only when it matters; no repeated disclaimers and no lines that announce what the member will not pretend. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_every_member_prompt_is_honest_without_hedging; core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_the_words_that_taught_the_boilerplate_are_gone; core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_a_gap_is_named_once_and_only_when_it_matters_for_every_speaker; core/tests/agent/test_member_behaviour.py::test_a_task_reports_what_it_made_by_its_title_and_what_is_not_done_once |

## Memory, attachments and team reads are not locked against the owner

Secrets were locked too tightly. What stays closed is a credential; what the owner asked for, or gave a member, is not.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-MEM-12 | 2026-11-14 | The owner corrected a street name in their address to a member, `memory_update` was refused as `unconfirmed_only` five times and the memory kept the typo. A turn that read outside content (a page, a search, a colleague) still marks what it writes and still never changes an entry the owner confirmed, but a `replace` the owner asked for in their own words of that private turn goes through: every span it writes (and every span it only takes out) stands in the owner's message, with up to two neighbouring characters, so that words they never said (a page's instruction, a rewrite beyond the correction) never pass, and a removal is no correction. The entry stays the owner's (no mark added); an outside claim they correct stays marked. A room, a colleague's work and a routine have no owner words, so the guard is unchanged there (R-MEM-03, R-MEM-07). | pytest | hard | core/tests/memory/test_files.py::test_the_owners_own_correction_in_a_turn_that_read_outside_content_replaces_a_confirmed_entry; core/tests/memory/test_files.py::test_an_owner_correction_never_confirms_the_outside_claim_it_edits_or_lets_other_words_in; core/tests/memory/test_files.py::test_outside_content_enters_marked_and_never_changes_what_the_owner_said |
| R-MEM-13 | 2026-11-14 | The owner corrected a fact a member had wrong, and its own instructions still stated the old fact days later: memory edits never reach the member's instructions. The doctrine every member reads (one sentence, beside the skill line) tells it to propose the fix of its own instructions with `member_update` on its own id when the owner corrects a fact they state: the owner's yes decides, like any change to a member, and nothing edits their data directly. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_skill_guidance.py::test_the_persona_carries_the_skill_line_beside_the_memory_doctrine; default prompt budget (`verify --contract`) |
| R-FLOOR-03 | 2026-11-14 | A member's local commands to read its own attachments (the files the owner attached in a conversation, stored in the app's `attachments` folder under their opaque ids) were refused as `protected_data` five times, because a read with a brace or a parenthesis (`if (Test-Path ...) { Get-Content ... }`) was taken for a write. A command or file tool that only reads such a file is no access to the app's data at all; one that changes it (a redirect, `Set-Content`, `Remove-Item`, a program that could) is still refused as before. The attachment's sidecar, every other file of the data folder (the database, the vault, keys, settings, `.env`), a sub-folder and the folder itself stay protected, and a command that names one of them beside an attachment is judged as before (R-FLOOR-01, R-FLOOR-02). | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_data_floor.py::test_a_member_reads_the_files_the_owner_attached_in_its_conversation; core/tests/agent/test_data_floor.py::test_an_attachment_is_still_not_written_by_a_command; core/tests/agent/test_data_floor.py::test_credentials_and_the_rest_of_the_data_folder_stay_protected_beside_attachments |
| R-ROUTINE-09 | 2026-11-14 | A team health check of a routine raised an approval card for every member it read (sixteen cards, two waited ten minutes), and the overnight runs were cancelled. In a routine's run, `member_manage` actions that only read (listing a member's routines, skills or commands, reading its settings: `READS`) ask for no card and are T1, not T3; every change keeps its tier and its exact-action card, and a destructive one its T3. | pytest | hard | core/tests/members/test_routine_reads.py::test_a_routines_run_reads_any_member_with_no_card_and_a_change_still_asks |

Automatic opened the Sound Mapper (whichever device Windows calls the default), and a quiet first 0.3 s sent it probing the other inputs in PortAudio's order, with Stereo Mix among them (live whenever anything plays) and "last good" remembered by a name whose 「3- 」 Windows renumbers. ## A file the owner attaches reaches the model as it is

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-ATTACH-RAW-01 | 2026-11-14 | A member (a Japanese teacher) given an `.html` file to copy its design received only the page's visible text, with `<style>` and `<script>` dropped, so that sending it again changed nothing. A file the owner attaches is the owner's own data. A text file (html, htm, css, js, ts, svg, xml, json, md, csv, code and any other text type the reader knows) reaches every speaker, API or CLI brain, a private conversation, a room and the background worker alike, as its source text, character for character: nothing is stripped, turned into "readable" text or JSON-escaped (the text follows a line of facts, not inside a JSON string). Root cause: `file_reader.read_text` ran every `.html` through a visible-text extractor (`ReadableHTML`) before the prompt was built, and the inline context then wrapped the result in JSON. The extractor is gone; a readable rendition beside the source was not added, because the source already holds the words and the rendition would spend the same budget twice. The untrusted-data wrapper stays: the prompt's own delimiter tags inside the file are neutralized, and its text is data, never instructions. A CLI brain also gets the stored original (`Files:` path), byte for byte. ## The first call after a wake is waited for; "your computer" is the member's

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COMP-FIRST-01 | 2026-11-14 | The first computer action of a member after the computer slept failed at least nine times (`docker_stopped`, `driver_configuration_failed`, `task_stopped`; a member told the owner 「電腦沒在運作」 while it ran) and 「再試一次」 made it work with nothing changed. The first call after a wake waits for the computer to be truly ready inside the one tool call, and looks again at the states that are only a computer on its way up. (1) `VmTools._awake` runs the member's start again, after a growing pause, at most `start_attempts` = 3 tries and `start_budget_s` = 150 seconds since the first, while it fails with `TRANSIENT_START` (`docker_stopped`, `unavailable`, `start_failed`, `health_timeout`); the repair of a lost connection does the same and may bring Docker Desktop back as the start may (it ran without that permission and answered `docker_stopped` for an engine that was only restarting). (2) `VmTools._configured` waits out `driver_configuration_failed` (a driver that has not finished starting refuses its first set-up calls), `connect_attempts` = 3 with a pause, before the repair. (3) A work item whose first call was cut short (its task cancelled while it waited for a sleeping computer) was marked stopped by the viewer's poll and every later call of that turn failed in milliseconds with `task_stopped`: a stop that was only read off the task (`Work.inferred`) is revived by the next live call of the same turn; a stop the owner said (a takeover stopped or expired) is not inferred and stays. (4) What is not transient is reported at once and never tried again: Docker not installed, Docker Desktop that could not be launched or did not come up in its two minutes, no image, an image that does not fit, no room, the owner holding control, the owner's Stop, a computer the member no longer has. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_computer_still_on_its_way_up_is_waited_for_inside_the_one_call; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_two_starts_in_a_row_that_are_not_ready_are_still_one_call; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_computer_that_never_comes_up_is_given_up_on_after_a_bounded_number_of_tries; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_the_wait_ends_at_its_time_bound_even_with_tries_left; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_real_failure_is_reported_at_once_and_never_tried_again; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_driver_that_is_not_configured_yet_is_waited_for; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_driver_that_is_never_configured_is_reported_after_a_bounded_number_of_tries; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_first_call_that_was_cut_short_does_not_stop_the_rest_of_its_turn; core/tests/computer/test_first_call.py::test_a_stop_the_owner_said_is_still_a_stop |

| R-CLI-SIGNIN-03 | 2026-11-14 | Eight turns on the Claude CLI failed `auth_expired` in three days, three of them 07:00 routines started together, and the owner had no warning. A subscription CLI that refuses its sign-in (`expired` or `signed_out`) is remembered (`CliBrains.sign_in_lost`) until a run of it is answered, a sign-in finishes in the app's console, its credential files change (a sign-in done anywhere; only their modification time is read) or thirty minutes pass. A routine run that would start meanwhile on that CLI is not started into the same refusal: it ends at once, unstarted, with the cause the first run told (reason `cli_login_skipped`, the same sentence 「Claude Code 的登入已過期。」 and the same turn failure `cause`), so the button of R-CLI-SIGNIN-02 stands under it and mends them all with one sign-in; after it a finished sign-in runs a failed task (a routine's run) again by the core in place of asking the routine's title as a question. Routine runs on one CLI start at least `ROUTINE_CLI_GAP_S` (10 s) apart, so the first run's refusal is known before the next starts. A task the owner handed over is never refused by this, and an answered one clears it. The allowance warning below 10% already exists for Claude (R-QUOTA-01: its `rate_limit_event` feeds `Quotas.record`); nothing new is asked of the CLI. | pytest, vitest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_a_refused_sign_in_is_remembered_until_something_says_it_is_back; core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_a_sign_in_that_finished_in_the_apps_own_console_clears_the_refusal; core/tests/background/test_routine_places.py::test_routines_after_a_refused_sign_in_are_not_run_into_it_and_say_the_cause_once; core/tests/background/test_routine_places.py::test_the_next_routine_asks_the_cli_again_once_a_sign_in_is_back; core/tests/background/test_routine_places.py::test_owner_chat_is_never_refused_by_the_routine_gate; app/src/main/cli-signin.test.ts::a finished sign-in runs a failed task again by the core (a routine's run), and asks the question again only for a reply with no task |

## Only credentials and payment secrets are secret

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SECRET-01 | 2026-11-14 | A member, a Japanese teacher, could not show an N1 study page three times and gave up because the page system took text in the content for a secret. Only real credentials and payment secrets are secret, and nothing else is blocked or masked as one: URLs, an ID or a number inside a URL, article text, hashes, file names, personal facts, a national ID number. `memory/scan.py` `find_secret` (and `redact`, which masks the same things in notes, skills, tool transcripts and a template's findings) finds: a key or token of a known shape (`sk-or-`/`sk-proj-`/`sk-ant-`, a bare `sk-` followed by one run of 20 or more letters and digits, `ghp_`, `github_pat_`, `xox`, `AKIA`, `AIza`, a JWT, `Bearer`, a PEM private key, a Telegram bot token); a value of eight characters or more, with a digit or the case changes of random letters, given to a word for a key (`api_key`, `token`, `secret`, `client_secret`, `access_token`, 金鑰, 密鑰, APIキー, …) by `:`, `=`, 是, 為, は or "is"; what a word for a password, a PIN, a one-time code or a CVV gives its value to (any value after `:`, `=`, "is" or "was"; after 是, 為 or は only a quoted value or one that begins in ASCII, so that パスワードは大切です, 暗証番号4桁を入力してください and 密碼是用來登入的 are sentences, not passwords); a card number (13 to 19 digits, first digit 2 to 6, Luhn, never the digits of a decimal, a word or an address's path or query); a bank account number (an IBAN, in one run or in groups of four, whose ISO 7064 check digits hold, or the 8 to 20 digits a word for a bank account gives its number to). A string that merely looks random is never a secret: the generic long-random-run check, the readable-path exception it needed (R-PAGE-05) and the Taiwan national ID number check are gone. A key of a known shape or a labelled credential inside a link (`?key=AIza…`, `?token=…`) is still one. The guard against steering a model (`find_injection`, applied to a memory at its write and when a turn reads it; never to a page, a note's text or a report) no longer refuses a link with a query string, or a picture at an address with none: the owner's links are shown, not obeyed. It still refuses an order to ignore the instructions, a line dressed as a system message, an order to send something to an address, a Markdown image whose address carries a query (a way to send data out) and invisible characters. Callers checked: memory writes and the prompt-time check (`memory/store.py`), the old-store merge (`memory/legacy.py`), `show_page`/`update_page` title, html, fallback text and tool data (`agent/pages.py checked_text`), report text, summary and link addresses (`agent/report_page.py`), notes, skills and the member's skill files (`bots/homes.py redact_secrets`), private tool transcripts (`memory/tool_events.py`), screen teaching (`computer/teaching.py`), a template's findings (`members/templates.py`) and the owner-private team report (`members/team_work.py`). Unchanged on purpose: the gate on a member exported as a recipe to be passed on (`members/template_author.py PRIVATE`: an email address, a path on the owner's disk, an address on their own network, a labelled account or owner name), the names a skill's folder may not hold (`members/capabilities.py _SECRET_FILE`), the vault, `request_secret` and the floor that keeps commands from the app's database and vault. | pytest | hard | core/tests/memory/test_scan.py::test_the_shapes_of_keys_and_tokens_are_secrets; core/tests/memory/test_scan.py::test_any_value_given_to_a_word_for_a_password_is_a_secret; core/tests/memory/test_scan.py::test_where_a_password_is_kept_is_not_one; core/tests/memory/test_scan.py::test_a_card_number_is_told_by_its_check_digit; ## Reordering the sidebar follows the pointer

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SIDE-03 | 2026-11-14 | Reordering the sidebar stalled. Measured, a drop sent one members.update per member of the folder (or one groups.update per folder), each waited for the core's answer (about 16 ms of its own work on a fresh database with fifteen members, `sync_homes` writing every member's home), each announced a change that read the whole roster again and drew the panel, the settings page and the conversation again: the row stood where it was dropped about 370 ms later in the mock core, three reads and five saves for one move. Now a row or a folder stands where it is dropped in the same task (`orderMs` under 150 ms, measured 10 to 60 ms), saved with one request (`members.update {group_id?, position}` or `groups.update {position}`; `position` is a place among every member but Raphael, `movedMember` in member-order.ts numbering as store.py `_move` does, pinned in core/tests/members/test_service.py). Only the moved row is a new node (and only when it changes folder: its face is kept); the rows and faces beside it, the folders and the conversation are the same nodes before and after. Moves wait for each other, and a read begun before a move or while it is saved is not drawn over it (R-SLIME-02). The core's answer and its change event that only confirm the order draw nothing but the column's patch (`withoutPlaces`: places, folders, the roster's order and revision are left out of the comparison, so no panel, settings page, conversation or Library read follows). A save that fails puts the column back, says why in the toast, and reads the core again. A move to where the row stands asks nothing; the hidden folder takes no drop. | vitest, pytest, look | hard | app/src/main/member-order.test.ts; app/src/main/pages/agent-members.test.ts::a row or a folder dropped on the sidebar; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_one_update_puts_a_member_in_a_folder_at_a_place_among_all_members_and_numbers_them_again; sidebar-reorder-members; sidebar-reorder-across; sidebar-reorder-folder; app/scripts/look/drag-probe.mjs; app/scripts/look/drag-probe.test.mjs; app/scripts/look/mock-ipc.test.mjs |

## The sidebar's lights

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SIDE-04 | 2026-11-14 | A member that was only thinking showed a light with nothing to read behind it, and the lights said too little of what each member needed. Cause: the core marked a thread unread at every `agent.turn` event of a turn that is written, and a running turn is written when it begins and again at each step (a routine's too, the moment it starts), so a member that only thought had a dot and nothing to read behind it. Now a turn that is still running marks its thread only once it has sent a message (a `reply`; a progress line is none), and the roster says what the unseen news is (`members.list` `unread_kind`, `rooms.list` `unread_kind`: `message`; `done` for a turn of a task or a routine that ended well, and for a group chat whose meeting ended with words the owner has not read; `failed` for a turn or task that ended in error, expiry, a limit or an interruption, the owner's own no or stop excepted; `failed` outranks `done` outranks `message`). A sidebar row (a member, a group chat, a pinned face, on the rail the face's corner) wears one still dot, the strongest of: orange (`--wait`, a question or an approval waits for the owner: `waiting` or the activity `blocked`; it stays until it is answered), red (`--err`, failed, or a run that failed unseen), green (`--ok`, done), white (`--text`, the theme's own foreground, a new message), none (nothing new: a member that thinks or works says nothing, its slime shows the work). The dot keeps the size and place it had (`--pa-dot`), and keeps its place under the pointer: a row draws no 「more」 (its right click is the menu, R-UI-44), so nothing makes room for one and the light and the words stand where every row's do (the row used to widen its right padding for the hidden button). It never animates, has no tooltip (its words would repeat the row) and names itself for a screen reader: 等你處理, 需要你看一下, 已完成, 有新訊息. Opening the conversation reads white, green and red at once (`lightOf(row, open)`; the core clears them with `members.read`), and orange stays while its question waits. The four colours keep 3:1 on every surface a row stands on, selected, hovered and pressed too (check-palette.mjs `sidebarLights`). The header's member picker shows one dot for all the other members, the strongest of their lights. | vitest, pytest, look | hard | app/src/main/sidebar-light.test.ts::a member that is thinking or working has none, nothing new to read; app/src/main/sidebar-light.test.ts::opening the conversation reads white, green and red, and an orange question stays while it waits; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_a_member_that_is_thinking_is_not_news_until_it_has_sent_something; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_a_routine_that_starts_is_not_news_and_one_that_ends_is_done; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_a_failed_turn_is_failed_news_and_a_reply_after_it_does_not_hide_it; core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_unread_kind_ranks_failed_over_done_over_message; core/tests/members/test_rooms.py::test_a_room_says_what_its_news_is_and_a_finished_meeting_is_done; sidebar-lights; sidebar-lights-rail; sidebar-lights-pinned; sidebar-lights-hover; sidebar-lights-hover-room; sidebar-lights-hover-pinned; sidebar-lights-thinking; sidebar-lights-open; sidebar-unread; app/scripts/check-palette.mjs |
| R-SIDE-05 | 2026-11-17 | Words the owner writes in a composer and has not sent yet stand in that conversation's sidebar row: its second line reads 草稿：<the words> (`members.draft`, one line as `plainLine` makes it) in place of the last line, for a member (its main conversation's draft, else another of its conversations') and for a group chat alike, the open conversation's own row too. They come with the draft as it is kept (`writeDraftWords`, 300 ms after the last key) and go when it goes (sent, emptied, given up); a quote alone or spaces are no words. A draft stands in place of a waiting question's line and its gold. Each kept or dropped draft is told by its key (`DRAFT_EVENT`), and the row's line is redrawn by the column's own patch, so typing touches no other row and nothing is drawn when the line is unchanged (`unsentWords` parses the kept drafts only when they changed). | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/member-column.test.ts::a member's row shows the words waiting in its composer, then its last line again once they go; app/src/main/member-column.test.ts::a draft stands in place of a waiting question's line, and a quote alone or spaces are no words; app/src/main/member-column.test.ts::typing in a composer redraws only its row's line in the mounted column; app/src/main/composer-drafts.test.ts::each kept or dropped draft is told by its key, and a row reads its member's or its group's words; sidebar-draft; sidebar-draft-cleared; sidebar-draft-room |
| R-SIDE-06 | 2026-11-17 | A member's sidebar line is the owner's message only until the member says something back. While its latest turn has no answer yet, the line is what the member last said in that turn after the question: a reply or a line it sent while it works (`AgentHistory.last_words`, kinds `reply` and `progress`, blank ones skipped), never its thinking or the conversation's own lines (a computer woken, a card). The answer, once written, is the line, as before. The roster and one member's view read it the same way, and each turn event already makes the column read its lines again. | pytest | hard | core/tests/members/test_service.py::test_the_column_shows_what_the_member_last_said_once_it_spoke_after_the_question |

| R-COMP-WAKE-01 | 2026-11-14 | A sleeping computer wakes in two ways only: a member's own computer tool call decides a task needs it (a turn, a routine or a background task alike), or the owner starts it. Opening a conversation or a panel never does, and reading a page never does: `web_read` falls back to the member's browser only when the computer is already awake; asleep, it returns the page as read with `READ_NEEDS_BROWSER` (or the failure with that instruction), so the member chooses `browser_open` itself. A start by a member's tool is never silent: one quiet line in that member's conversation, an event and never a message of the member's (R-TEXT-08; `vm_tools.py::WOKE`, a progress line where no conversation takes notices) names the member and why (a page, a command, or using the computer) in every interface language, with no toast; the member's panel says nothing of it, since the computer is the third tab only (R-PANEL-06; the core still reports `woken` in the viewer's status, cleared when it sleeps or the owner starts it, and the app no longer reads it). | R-DICT-CLEAN-01 | 2026-11-17 | Dictation cleanup is in no one language. Nothing before or after the model edits a dictation's words by a list of one language's words (no filler remover, no remover of an unspoken question particle, no stop rule for one language), and every dictation goes to the model however short: whether "Um I think so.", 「嗯我覺得可以。」 or "Send it Monday I mean Tuesday." holds a filler or a self-correction depends on the language, which only the model reads, so there is no shortcut past it for a short clear sentence (a short dictation costs one cleanup call). Without the model (no key, an error, a suspicious reply) the words are pasted as speech-to-text wrote them, obvious stutters removed, and the dictation says it was not cleaned. The template (`prompts/cleanup_system.md`) names what to remove by kind, in no one language, and says in terms that the model adds no word, particle, question or question mark and that a request stays a request; no check after the model enforces that, and it is unmeasured on real dictation. Each of the first three chosen languages, in the order of the setting, adds its worked examples (`prompts/cleanup_examples.<subtag>.md`, one for every language the settings offer; mainland Chinese takes the Chinese ones in Simplified character by character, so they use words both regions write alike). The fake drivers of the tests took any argument. Their measured fixture now retains every validation keyword, removing only descriptive annotations. Browser fakes also return the measured authorization_host_failed refusal for target-only get_browser_state and refuse browser_navigate outside http/https/about; schema validation alone does not express these runtime constraints. A set-up call carries only what `cua-driver describe` names (`enabled` and `session` for the cursor, `max_image_dimension` and `session` for the config). A driver session label is never used twice: the driver of a computer that kept running refuses a label that has ended, so a core started again does not count from 1 once more. `npm run verify -- --computer` makes the product's own connection twice on a real computer (the second as a core started again) and calls a tool through it. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_latency.py::test_window_reads_are_bounded_and_driver_images_and_cursor_are_configured; core/tests/computer/test_latency.py::test_a_core_started_again_never_reuses_a_driver_session_label; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_driver_check_makes_the_products_own_connection_with_arguments_the_driver_accepts; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_driver_check_fails_when_the_driver_refuses_a_set_up_argument; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_driver_check_starts_no_driver_process_in_another_owners_container |
| R-COMP-DRIVER-02 | 2026-11-13 | Found with real members on the shared computer: a member's first use of its computer started its CLI turn again with "The computer tool catalog changed", the first call's result was lost, and the member went on guessing window and tab ids. The list kept with the code (`driver_schemas.json`) was older than the driver in the image: all 30 tools had other parameters, some the driver refuses, so asking the driver replaced every one. The kept list is the pinned driver's own `tools/list` for the offered tools, and it and a running driver's list are read by one function (`vm_catalog.definition`): asking the pinned driver changes nothing a member was offered, to the byte, and what a call is checked against before the driver is asked is the driver's own schema. A driver that lists something else is still taken, the one time the list changes. Found beside it: a description is cut at 400 characters and every computer tool opened with the same approval rule, more than twice that long, so all thirty-three read as its same opening words and none said what it did (a member sent PowerShell to its Linux shell). A computer tool's description is shown whole (`PluginTool.whole`), opens with what the tool is for and carries the approval rule after it; the working guidance that was cut off unseen is in the member's instructions, once. The driver's own description is not offered (its parameters' words are). The shared computer's sentence is on the four tools it is about. Every request of every member carries these tools, so their descriptions' total length is bounded by a test. `npm run verify -- --computer` compares the running driver's list with the kept one (words and order included), asks the driver through `VmTools` and requires the offer unchanged, and writes the list beside its report (`driver-tools.json`), from which `generate_driver_tools.py` writes both kept files. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_catalog_stays.py; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_live_schema_check_requires_the_kept_list_to_be_the_drivers_own; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_live_schema_check_fails_when_asking_the_driver_changes_the_offer |
| R-COMP-DRIVER-03 | 2026-11-16 | The app's log held 24 lines `Failure unknown: Elapsed: N seconds.` in three days: twelve calls (each written twice) of `browser_open`, `browser_tabs`, `get_browser_state`, `get_desktop_state` and `list_windows`, in 0.03 to 70.78 s, whose step reason was a bare `error`. That is the computer layer's own catch-all: an exception that was neither the driver's answer nor a timeout, a lost connection or a refusal was kept as `error` with no text and no line of the log, the read's elapsed time was then all the result said, and the classifier took it for the reason. The member read "The call failed (error). Elapsed: 70.78 s."; the owner's step "The cause is unknown". Now such a call is kind `computer_fault` (classified `tool_broken`), its text says it was Raphael's own computer layer, which exception, that whether anything was done is unknown and to read the window or page first; the log writes the tool, the exception's type and the file, line and function it was raised in (never its message); the owner's step says, in all fifteen languages, that it was Raphael's own computer tool and not the computer or the page. A failed read says why first, and its elapsed time never stands alone. One driver connection serves several calls, and a call that ran out of time closes it for all: a call between two requests then met no process and failed with an `AttributeError` (one way into the catch-all, inferred from the code, not measured); it is now a closed connection (`ConnectionError`), which the computer repairs and tells as such. A failed call is written to the log and to self_check's last ten once, by the loop that reads it, not twice. | pytest, vitest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_core_faults.py::test_a_screen_read_that_breaks_in_the_core_says_where_and_is_no_bare_elapsed_time; core/tests/computer/test_core_faults.py::test_a_read_that_answers_still_says_how_long_it_took; core/tests/computer/test_core_faults.py::test_a_request_on_a_connection_another_call_closed_is_a_closed_connection; app/src/main/timeline.test.ts::a computer step that broke in the core says it was Raphael's own tool, not an unknown cause |

| R-COMP-SIGNIN-01 | 2026-11-13 | Signing in to Google at a member's computer, so that the members have the owner's account, met \"Couldn't sign you in. This browser or app may not be secure", a yellow bar about `--no-sandbox`, and "Restore pages?". The members' browser is one a person signs in with. A browser started with `--remote-debugging-port=0` tells every page it is automated (`navigator.webdriver`), which is what Google refuses: the seat browser is started with `--disable-blink-features=AutomationControlled`, never with `--enable-automation` or headless. `--test-type` keeps the unsupported-flag bars away (the seat browser is started from its binary and never read the Debian wrapper's flags file). A computer is stopped by ending its processes, so before the browser's first start its profile is marked as closed (`exit_type` Normal) and the browser is started with `--hide-crash-restore-bubble`; a Preferences file that is not what the browser writes is left as it is. The same step keeps passwords, cards and addresses out of the profile (`credentials_enable_service`, the password manager and autofill off): every member reads that one profile and a password the browser keeps can be shown again on its own page, so what an owner types at a seat is not offered to be saved ("Save password?" was, in the first real run on Chrome); sign-ins stay as cookies and saved values belong to the vault. The same holds as the browser's own managed policy, whatever a profile says or whether it could be written, and by that policy the browser is never signed in to as a browser and never syncs: Google's own Chrome offers an owner who signs in to Google on a page to turn on sync, and their own saved passwords, history and bookmarks would come down into the profile every member reads (found in review before any owner signed in). `npm run verify -- --computer` reads the running browser's own flags, its profile and its policy and opens a page in it whose title says what `navigator.webdriver` is ("shared browser is a person's"). The shared computer has one browser: its desktop's Browser icon, and a link another program opens, give a window of the members' own browser on the screen the pointer is on, because a second browser with a profile of its own is where an owner would sign in for nobody (a computer of a member's own keeps the desktop's browser, and Google's Chrome there is given the flags Debian's Chromium reads from /etc/chromium.d). Stated limit: whether Google lets a sign-in through is Google's decision at the time; no test signs in to Google, and the owner's own sign-in is the proof. | pytest, computer acceptance | hard | core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_members_browser_is_one_a_person_signs_in_with; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_profile_whose_browser_was_ended_is_marked_closed_and_any_other_file_is_left; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_shared_computers_browser_icon_opens_the_members_own_browser; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_members_browser_is_never_signed_in_to_as_a_browser_and_keeps_nothing_typed |
| R-COMP-OWNER-INPUT-01 | 2026-11-13 | The enlarged screen of a seat (a sign-in page) said the owner was in control while their input did not arrive. Every check of an owner's hold had looked at what the members were refused, none at whether the owner's own mouse and keys arrive. In an earlier build the card's take-over did not leave the watching state of an enlarged screen and the pill read the lease alone; both have since changed. The core's own path is proven and kept proven: `npm run verify -- --computer` sends a click and a typed line as the app's page sends them (the session's relay path, on the connection made while watching) and requires that they reach the held seat and reach nothing before the hold or after the handback ("shared owner input"); the `shared-computer` native journey has a member ask for a sign-in at its seat, takes the seat from the conversation's card and types into the page, and the member continues once.
