## Computer image, layer 6

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COMP-PICTURE-01 | 2026-11-12 | On layer 5 the desktop was one flat colour for two reasons, both measured in a real container. The session started a watcher of its own without the owner's picture, and the core, finding a watcher running, left: the picture was copied into the computer and never shown. And the image had no gdk-pixbuf loader for SVG, so the town (five SVG drawings) was never drawn either. The core is the one place a watcher starts: it ends the one before it and starts its own, with the owner's picture when there is one (R-UI-95) and the default picture when there is none (`day.jpg` or `night.jpg`: R-UI-92); the session starts none. A picture that was changed, removed or put back is on the desktop at the computer's next start, with no restart of the computer or the app; a restart that ended the watcher starts it again. The image installs `librsvg2-common` and its build fails without the loader (the default pictures need no SVG loader since they are JPEGs; the image keeps it). `npm run verify -- --computer` reads the screen: the picture's own two colours while there is a picture, many colours and `last-image` naming `day.jpg` or `night.jpg`, never one flat colour, once there is none, the picture again when it is put back, and exactly one watcher. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_vm.py::test_a_changed_or_removed_picture_is_put_up_at_the_next_start_of_a_running_computer; core/tests/computer/test_vm.py::test_start_installs_the_decorative_wallpaper_once_per_start_and_target; core/tests/computer/test_wallpaper.py::test_the_session_starts_no_watcher_of_its_own; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_image_installs_screen_views_and_preserves_the_single_token_port; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_the_desktop_picture_verdict_tells_the_owner_picture_the_default_and_a_flat_colour_apart |
| R-COMP-KEYS-01 | 2026-11-12 | Measured in a real container: the desktop had four workspaces and every default key, and `ctrl+F2` moved the whole computer to another workspace, so every window was gone from the screen; `ctrl+F4`, an ordinary "close this tab", did the same. A computer has one workspace, and no key acts on the whole desktop: the workspace keys, show desktop, log out, lock, xkill and the display settings are unbound for any number of screens. With more than one screen the keys that walk over every screen's windows (alt+Tab and its kin) and every key that starts a program (it opens where the one pointer is) are unbound too. A window's own keys stay (close, maximize, move, resize, tile), and one screen keeps alt+Tab and its program keys. On one screen, keys that cannot be changed are reported and never hold the screen back. With more than one screen, every removal is attempted despite individual failures, then the helper reads the bindings and workspace count again; remaining shortcut_removals bindings, a workspace count other than 1, or a failed required query raises into the existing retry before a new ready mark is written. The two session-default override waits retain their 20-second limits. `verify --computer` presses ctrl+F2, ctrl+F4 and ctrl+alt+d on a real computer with a window open and finds it shown where it was. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_one_screen_loses_the_keys_that_act_on_the_whole_desktop_and_keeps_its_own; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_several_screens_also_lose_the_keys_that_reach_every_screen; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_the_keys_are_taken_away_once_the_session_has_copied_them_and_one_workspace_is_left; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_keys_that_cannot_be_changed_never_hold_the_screens_back |
| R-COMP-XLOSS-01 | 2026-11-12 | Measured in a real container: a one-screen computer whose X server was lost never got it back, kept its ready mark, answered `ready` for good and listed no window, because only a clipped view (more than one screen) died with the server and told the helper. The helper looks at the X server itself, for any number of screens, without starting a process; when it is gone the mark goes, the computer is not called ready, and the product's next start restarts it. `verify --computer` ends the X server of a one-screen and of the largest computer, sees the mark go within 5 seconds and the status turn not ready, then starts the computer and finds an X server, the mark and the panel. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_a_lost_x_server_takes_the_mark_away_for_any_number_of_screens; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_the_look_at_the_x_server_starts_no_process |

| R-COMP-LAYER-01 | 2026-11-12 | A computer of layer 5, the shape every released computer has today (one screen that says its count, 3 GiB, two CPUs), is a previous layer exactly like 3 and 4: its next start or update rebuilds it on the new image and keeps its workspace, and erasing it needs no new image. ## Computer image screens, stage A

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COMP-SCREEN-01 | 2026-11-11 | Layer 5 can give one container 1 to 6 real outputs, each with a panel and its own clipped view through a screen token on the one published port. One screen uses the main VNC server directly. Readiness requires `/dev/shm/raphael-screens.ready` on the fresh-per-start tmpfs: the mark says this boot's screens are set up, stays while a view or the helper restarts and goes only when the layout is lost, and a viewer reports a refused connection itself. Tokens stay under `/run/user/1000`. Supervisor never gives up on the helper, and a running computer whose driver is healthy but whose mark is still absent after 20 start polls is restarted once under the repair guards. A computer of a previous layer (3 or 4) is this owner's own for stop, restart, update, repair, reset and member-data deletion before it has ever been upgraded. A `Target` refuses a screen outside 1..screens..6, and the layout request has one 15 s deadline. The helper starts `/usr/bin/X0tigervnc` in the foreground, ends leftovers before views start for any count, and waits boundedly for all six ports to clear. Listener checks parse `/proc/net/tcp` without VNC connections. Supervisor lacks CAP_KILL and only restarts what exits; the helper ends its own children, and nothing may call `supervisorctl stop` or `restart` in a computer. A new layout uses a 1x1 update request then moves the pointer to screen 1 only after success; matching monitors skip both. Multiple screens disable only dock shadows, writing only when different without a panel restart. Existing per-bot and shared targets remain screen 1 of 1 in stage A. What the model is told matches what it can call: its prompt says that with no computer tool listed it says so plainly and promises none. No member is asked whether it may have a computer: nothing asks and no grant exists. A member uses its computer when its work needs it, as it uses any tool, under the permission mode the owner chose: there is no request_computer tool, no 「允許 {member} 使用電腦」 card, no Start or take-back button, no computer.revoke method and no grant table in use (`computer_grants` stays in the schema unused: no migration). Every member, routines and room speakers included, is offered its computer's tools whenever computer tools are on and Docker Desktop is installed on this PC (or a remote host is chosen); a PC without Docker offers none and leaves the owner's own desktop tool on offer. What protects the owner is unchanged: sandbox gestures pass the screening and need the lease, a saved sign-in, contact, card or dataset still obeys the safety rules and the mode, fill_card is T3, the worker still cannot drive the owner's own desktop, payment holds, takeover, the owner's hold and a changed target still refuse a call. In 先詢問 and 計畫 sandbox gestures were never asked after the first yes and are not now. The owner's press on the screen (not a preview, not a status read) starts a stopped computer; a member's own call may start Docker Desktop on this PC, a remote host never. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_member_computer_access.py::test_every_member_is_offered_the_computer_without_a_question_and_nothing_starts; | R-AG-MGMT-02 | 2026-11-05 | Every destructive management action requires one exact-action owner approval in every permission mode, including bypass. Delete, clear, disconnect, membership removal, primary transfer and retention shortening are firm T3; cards name targets by name and the removal scope in words (in the owner's language in every locale, no ID, no JSON). The firm changes a member asks for in one turn are not asked one behind another (the second was not even proposed until the first was answered, and a card nobody saw ran out): they are held until the turn is in and asked as one card, a line each, one yes and one no. So is every other administrative change or reading that the mode or a rule asks about (fourteen proposals are one card, not fourteen), in plain words with the asking member named on the card's first line when it is not Raphael; the team proposals the owner edits (members and folders) keep their own card. In the 全部允許 mode only the firm ones are asked, as before. The yes is for exactly that list (its digest and its lines), each change is carried out in order under its own part of it and once, one that fails does not stop the others and is said plainly to the owner and to the member, and a change proposed after the card was shown is a card of its own. The yes of a held reading brings its result to the member's turn. Credentials, sign-in, safety, permission, spending, access approvals, takeover and device settings remain owner-only. Models change only on an explicit owner request. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/src/raphael_core/management.py; core/tests/members/test_management.py; core/tests/members/test_management_batch.py::test_three_changes_are_one_card_with_a_line_each_and_one_yes_carries_out_all; core/tests/members/test_management_batch.py::test_one_failing_change_is_said_plainly_and_the_others_still_run; core/tests/members/test_management_batch.py::test_declining_declines_all_of_them; core/tests/members/test_management_batch.py::test_a_yes_covers_exactly_the_list_that_was_shown; core/tests/members/test_admin_proposals.py::test_fourteen_readings_of_one_turn_are_one_card_and_the_yes_brings_their_results; core/tests/members/test_admin_proposals.py::test_several_changes_of_one_turn_are_one_card_and_one_yes_makes_them_in_order; core/tests/members/test_admin_proposals.py::test_the_card_names_the_member_that_asks; admin-delete-many; admin-delete-many-approved |

A member's turn holds such changes until it is in and asks them after it, so a CLI brain's tool call is never held open for a card (a CLI call cannot wait long), on every brain. ## Slime town and computer preparation

R-UI-93 supersedes R-CARD-01's no-face rule only inside the booting computer screen's desktop-boot-face. Card headings, bodies and other card kinds retain the no-face contract.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-92 | 2026-11-03 | The default picture of every computer and every preview is one of two original abstract pictures made for Raphael, the light `day.jpg` from 06:00 to 17:59 and the dark `night.jpg` from 18:00 to 05:59. The computer picks by its own clock, which runs in the owner's zone (vm.refresh_timezone, from the zone the window sends with members.viewing); the preview picks by the window's clock, the same zone, at the same hours, and turns while it is open. Both are zoomed from the centre to fill the 1280x900 screen, and app/public/wallpapers holds the same bytes. | R-PC-01 | 2026-11-13 | The owner's own screen (the run on their desktop, the fixed commands that open, lock, copy and change the volume, and the fast router that picks those) is in reach of a quick question and of nothing else: not an ordinary conversation, typed or said, Raphael's or a member's, not a room, an exchange between members, a message from a phone, Telegram or Slack, a task or a routine, and no permission mode, 全部允許 included, reopens it. Such a turn is offered none of those tools, the fast router is not asked to route it, and a call that names one anyway is refused. What it shows on a screen (a website, YouTube, a program, clicks, typing) it shows on the member's own computer, and when that is not available the member says so and never falls back to the owner's screen. In a quick question the owner's PC is the default for everything, commands and what is seen alike, and the member's own computer is offered beside it for a request whose words name it (用你的電腦, your computer, in any language): the model reads the owner's words and picks the tool, the prompt says which is the default and how the owner names the other, and no word matched in code takes either computer away (such words only skip the fast router's shortcut, which can use the owner's PC alone). | R-PC-04 | 2026-11-16 | As Claude Code's Read, Glob and Grep, members read, find and search the files of the owner's own computer (`owner_files`: read_file, glob, grep) from every turn and task, typed, said, from a phone, a room, Telegram, Slack, a task or a routine, in every permission mode, without a card: the whole computer the owner's account can open. Unlike Claude Code, which a person drives at the keyboard, Raphael also takes messages from phones and rooms and reads web pages, so the places that hold keys and passwords are kept from these tools whatever is asked, a link to one included: SSH, GPG and cloud keys, the keychain and Windows' credential stores, browsers' profiles, password managers, `.env` files and private keys. Raphael's own data keeps the data floor. A walk skips app data, caches and packages unless it starts inside them (on a Mac each app's container would ask the owner). | R-PC-06 | 2026-11-16 | Outside a quick question nothing is shown on the owner's screen by a command either: a local command or a CLI brain's own command whose purpose is to show something there (a program with a window started, a URL, a file or a shortcut opened in its default program: Start-Process, saps, start, cmd /c start, explorer with a path or a URL, Invoke-Item, ii, rundll32 url.dll, Process.Start, a URL, a shortcut or a document typed as the command, a windowed program such as notepad or a browser, and on a Mac open and an AppleScript that brings an app to the front) is refused in every permission mode, 全部允許 included, without a card, and the member is told in words to open it on its own computer, or to run a program directly when only what it prints is needed. A command that shows nothing passes (git status, dir, Get-ChildItem, python script.py, npm test, a URL fetched by curl or Invoke-WebRequest). This stops a model that picked the wrong computer: it reads the command's words, it is no shell parser and no defence against someone set on getting round it, and the approval card stays the gate of what runs (`computer/on_screen.py`). Public paraphrases of early product decisions. Later rules take precedence. Each row is one requirement. Explicit conflicts remain `human` with both versions. `hard` violations fail judge regardless of score; `human` needs product/native acceptance. This ledger records requirements, not claims of compliance. Mechanical references use `repository/path::exact test title`.

Home no longer exists (R-UI-114, R-UI-43, R-UI-03 and R-UI-04 are retired): the app opens on Raphael's
conversation and the calendar opens only when asked (R-NAV-02, R-SIDE-02). The designs of Home below are
kept only as the ledger's history.

| R-UI-35 | 2026-11-07 | Onboarding after the welcome is three choices: the AI tools, the services and the abilities members share. The guide is one card of a designed size in the middle of the window, with no progress bar and no side: each step is one calm centred column, a title with two small aria-hidden slimes floating by it (gone below 640px wide), the choice, and under the choice two round buttons together in the middle of the card: at the left the way back and at the right the way on, each a 48 px circle with one filled triangle that points where it goes (the same drawing, turned round for the way back), 12 px apart. Neither has words, so each is named for assistive tech and in its tooltip: 上一步, and 下一步 (the last step's way on is named 開始使用 and holds a check instead of a triangle: it finishes, and nothing comes after it). The way on is a circle of the solid accent; the way back is on a quiet grey, one step lighter under the pointer; neither has an edge; the way back takes the app's focus ring and comes before the primary in the tab order. The pair stands in the same place on every step, never stacks and is one control tall, so the step's content keeps the room (the guide's own sizes are the one exception to the shared button height). Two stacked pills are not used, because stacking takes room that the choice of AI tools and of services needs; neither is a worded pill at each end of the row. The welcome is its one wide pill in the middle and nothing else. The guide is the first run's only: Settings has no entry that opens it again. The tools (accounts, the key, the providers) and the services (under a search box) are tiles in a grid of three where it fits, each one row (a taller tile broke an email in two): a mark in a soft square, a name over one quiet line that never wraps, its control on the right inside the tile, and a black edge with a check when it is on; a service tile that only signs in is one whole button. Without a Composio key the services step offers Composio's card first and then only the apps that need none; its search finds every app, n8n included, and sets it up in its tile or the store's own form. The abilities are Settings › 工具's own rows and dialogs, each saying which service it uses and opening the choice of service, key and model. Controls are real buttons and switches. This supersedes the earlier ability checklist and its per-ability setup screens. | look, judge | hard | features/onboarding.md; app/scripts/look/audit.test.mjs::keeps an onboarding choice's control on the right of its name, inside its card (R-UI-35); onboarding-tools; onboarding-services; onboarding-abilities; onboarding-abilities-choice; onboarding-back-hover; onboarding-back-focus; settings-owner; app/src/main/pages/settings-view.test.ts::General groups its sections: you, appearance and Raphael, the bots live elsewhere, and no way back into the guide; app/src/main/onboarding/mount.test.ts::the foot is the welcome's pill alone, and then on every step a pair of round buttons, the way back before the way on; app/src/main/onboarding/mount.test.ts::the back button goes back one step, and the way on keeps its name |

## Escape, deletion and confirmation cards

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-UI-46 | 2026-10-28 | First Escape arms the visible task Stop with Esc and a localized hint; second within 1500ms cancels once. Other keys, input, clicks, blur, expiry or target changes clear it. Stop shares Send's component and color tokens. The ordinary desktop orb omits Stop and uses the orb as its focused Escape target; permission/question cards retain their actions. While recording, the composer's button is the send glyph (R-UI-60) and a click on it releases, transcribes and sends; only Escape cancels. | vitest, look, judge | hard | app/src/shared/cancel-gesture.test.ts::only a second distinct Escape in 1500ms cancels the same target; features/conversation.md; features/team-and-rooms.md; features/dictation.md |

| R-UI-48 | 2026-10-28 | Desktop YES/NO has an opaque shared card, a plain intent title, wrapped clamped expandable details and separate non-overlapping actions at native sizes in both themes. The YES of a question that cannot be undone (T3) is one press, as every other, never a press held for 0.6 s with a line filling under its word. The protections that cost the owner nothing stay: the buttons are disabled while an answer is sent, a second press answers nothing, and no button takes the focus by itself. The card says what will happen in the owner's language in plain words: the heading is the effect, the lines under it name the target, with no ID, no JSON and no fragment of another language. | vitest, look, judge | hard | app/src/overlay/overlay-style.test.ts::confirmation details have an opaque card surface and a separate scrollable expansion; app/src/overlay/overlay.test.ts::YES of a T3 question is one click too, answered once, with no hold in between; app/src/main/pages/agent-cards.test.ts::one press of 是 answers all of them, once, and a second press does nothing; admin-delete-approval; admin-delete-approved; features/dictation.md |

| R-VC-03 | 2026-11-11 | The dictation key always wins. Once the words of a question said to the overlay (Alt+Space) are in, it goes on as a turn of its own, so Right Alt starts a dictation at once while its answer is worked on, waits for a yes or no, or is on the overlay, exactly as if nothing were there; the model call is never cancelled by it, and stopping the question stays what it was (Esc or the overlay's Stop, which also drops an answer that waits). While the owner dictates the overlay shows the dictation alone: an answer that arrives meanwhile, or was showing when the recording began, is held, and once the dictation is finished and its words are typed it is shown for its ordinary time. If a newer quick question was asked meanwhile only the newest answer shows; every answer is in the quick conversation as ever. A held answer is kept in memory only, so it is dropped when the app quits or the core restarts. Right Alt stays ignored only while the question itself is recorded or transcribed (the core has one session at a time), and Alt+Space does not bury a yes or no question that waits. The spoken yes or no, its 30 seconds, the recording limit and the learning of corrections do not change. | test, native | hard | app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::dictation_works_while_a_question_said_to_the_overlay_is_answered; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::dictation_works_while_a_question_said_to_the_overlay_waits_for_a_yes_or_no; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::an_answer_that_arrives_during_a_recording_is_held_and_shown_once_its_words_are_typed; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::an_answer_on_the_overlay_when_a_dictation_begins_is_shown_again_after_it; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::a_newer_quick_question_replaces_the_answer_that_is_held; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::the_answer_of_an_older_quick_question_never_follows_a_newer_question; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::a_stopped_quick_question_shows_nothing_whatever_it_answers_later; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::esc_stops_a_held_answer_after_the_recording_that_took_the_overlay; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::the_core_restarting_drops_an_answer_that_is_held; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::a_held_answer_is_dropped_when_the_app_quits; overlay-held-answer; features/dictation.md |

## Member computer hosts

R-COMP-HOST supersedes the shared-desktop default: every member lazily receives a
separate computer and browser profile. Sign-ins remain per member. A team folder at
`/home/cua/team` is shared; members sign in separately. Settings offers one shared
location dropdown (This computer / Another computer), with an SSH config alias for
the latter. A configured alias selects the remote engine; otherwise installed local
Docker is used. With neither, the rest of the app works and the existing computer
settings explains the missing runtime and offers installation or another host.

Remote host check (R-COMP-HOST-01). "Check host" reports the truth about what a real start
needs, and for every failure says which of these is missing and shows the one command or config snippet that fixes it,
copyable, with no wizard or new screen: an alias in this PC's ssh config (`ssh_alias_unknown`: a `Host` snippet to
fill in), the host key accepted once (`ssh_hostkey`: `ssh <alias>`; `ssh_hostkey_changed`: `ssh-keygen -R <host>`),
key login without a password prompt (`ssh_key`: `ssh-keygen -t ed25519` and `ssh-copy-id <alias>`, or the PowerShell
pipe on Windows), a reachable SSH server (`ssh_unreachable`), Docker installed (`docker_missing`), `docker` on the
non-interactive PATH (`docker_path`), Docker running (`docker_stopped`) and usable by that account (`docker_access`).
A reachable host with a running Docker and no image yet is healthy: the real start pulls and builds the image on the
host's own engine, so `image_pending` is a note ("the first start prepares it, roughly 5 to 15 minutes"), never a
failure and never a command. An image built for another architecture (`architecture`) is the only image failure, and
its fix removes that image on the host. Every command shown is pasted on this PC, reaches the host through
`ssh <alias>` or sets up this PC's own ssh, and never builds anything from the app's sources, which an installed app
does not have. Docker Desktop on a Mac cannot be installed over ssh, so that class shows words and no command.

Remote Docker uses a validated `ssh://alias`, no published viewer port, named
workspace/team volumes on that engine, and a temporary random loopback SSH viewer
forward. It never starts Docker Desktop on this PC. Local idle-stop remains 15
minutes; remote desktops have no idle-stop. New v4 containers have a 3 GiB memory/swap cap
(not a reservation) and two CPUs. Local starts keep at most two running owned computers, counting
old incompatible containers without stopping them. Only a compatible, longest-idle
computer with no work or owner lease may be evicted; otherwise
report capacity. Remote hosts have no such limit.

The member viewer has one dark neutral frame in both themes, the member's name,
and a collapse control at the top right. It preserves the current stream and
handback nonce. The image has a plain wallpaper and one small bottom-centred dock
with Browser, Files and Terminal. No browser starts with the desktop. Task teaching
is outside scope. AT-SPI and websockify stay until real driver/viewer compatibility
can be established.

## Connect apps in one view, and one colour for everything that floats

Connect Apps is one view, not separate connected and store tabs: the connected apps stand above the recommended ones, the recommended ones are filtered by category (all, communication, productivity, development), and no count of the catalogue is shown. Every floating surface, a dialog included, follows one set of colours.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-FLOAT-01 | 2026-11-12 | Everything that floats over the window (a dialog, a menu and its submenu, a popover, a floating card, a tooltip) stands on one surface with one edge, in the dark theme: the window and sidebar are #0e0e0e, what floats is #1f1f1f (`--float`) with a 1 px #2b2b2b edge (`--float-edge`), except a dialog, which is the darker middle layer (`--dialog`, R-FLOAT-03); a control standing on it (a chip, a 連結 button, a field, a card inside) is filled #282828 (`--float-ctl`, hovered #303030), a hovered menu row is #292929 (`--float-hover`), a divider inside is the edge colour, the chosen chip is a light fill with dark words (`--float-sel`, `--float-on-sel`), and the dimmed window behind a dialog reads about #050505. The light theme keeps its character: a white surface, a #e5e5e5 edge, a light grey control (#ececec) and hover (#eaeaea), a black chosen chip with white words. The surfaces say it once (`:where(.menu, .modal, .mb-dialog, ...)` in styles.css points the ordinary tokens at the floating ones inside them), `--menu` is `var(--float)`, and a new floating surface joins that list and uses `--float` and `--float-edge`. Pages, the sidebar, the thread, the composer and a toast (the inverse of the page) keep their own colours. Every text grey keeps 4.5:1 on `--float`, `--float-ctl` and their hover and pressed fills, the status colours and the delete question's red hold on them (check-palette.mjs), and a text button on a floating surface is framed by its fill (R-UI-18). | vitest, lint, look | hard | app/src/main/floating.test.ts::every floating surface stands on the floating tokens and says it in one scope; app/src/main/floating.test.ts::the tokens are fixed greys in the dark theme and a white surface in the light one; scripts/check-palette.mjs; connect-apps; connect-apps-more; member-delete-ask; context-menu-member; composer-model-small; member-maker; automations-detail-own; model-choices-answer; profile |

## Conversation-managed connections

R-MCP-CHAT-01: a custom MCP server is an editable conversation card. Public configuration and secret names may be proposed; values cross only the existing masked secret card into the vault. Initialization and discovery must succeed for the exact configuration before owner enable. Stdio testing uses the existing local-command consent and setting; allow-all skips yes, never a forbid, disabled local commands or secret-store protection. Saving a secret alone does not claim the task resumed. Stop rejects late discovery.

R-MCP-CHAT-02: connections_list and manage_connection use the existing connection and connector services. App/tool enable, disable, disconnect and reconnect apply to API, authenticated CLI MCP and background offers and to the check immediately before execution. A stale alias receives a refusal explaining the new state. Disconnect follows the shared mutation approval. No extra Settings page or preference is added.

Offline evidence: core/tests/integrations/test_chat_connections.py, core/tests/agent/test_connection_management.py, core/tests/background/test_connection_offers.py and app/src/main/mcp-proposal.test.ts. Selected real-entry scenes and limitations are mapped in features/connections-mcp.md. These rules extend R-AG-35 and R-OWNER-02.

## Shared conversation cards

| ID | Date | Rule | Verification | Level | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-COMP-SEAT-01 | 2026-11-12 | One computer serves every member, each on its own screen, signed in once. Shared mode means one six-screen container on each selected host, with a durable first-use seat per member. Shared is the default on this PC's Docker; nothing in the app chooses the mode, because a feature of the app has no on/off switch. On a remote host (computer_ssh_alias) the default stays a computer per member until shared mode has been run on a real remote host: LocalVm.mode is the one place that decides, and a saved computer_vm_mode always wins. Per-bot retains its own container and lease behavior. A member's own get_browser_state that names no window (nothing at all, the browser's process alone, or ids this member never bound) reads the one browser window on its own screen, and the reply names the real target and tabs: one browser serves every seat, so its process names no window, and real members at a seat sent exactly these and gave up when told their own window was not on their screen. It is that one read only: input still names its window, a target the member did bind is judged as before, and what the core reads for a member (a private page's state, a field before a saved value is typed) is the tab it asked for or nothing. A process alone, on any other call, is answered with what to pass, not with "not on this member's screen". Seat keys keep tools, routes, leases, sessions, active work, holds and private pages separate. Unit identity owns physical locks, lifecycle, capacity, activity and caches. Any seat's active work, hold or busy lease protects physical actions. Every native driver call and every call dispatched with delivery_mode=foreground takes the unit input lock. Other seats' background page calls, dialog inspection, reads and commands continue during an owner hold; foreground page input waits then returns computer_busy. The held seat refuses all member calls. A seventh member selects the least recently used idle seat under seat_lock, revokes its old holder and disconnects its driver and viewers; it publishes the new holder on that exact screen only after a successful window list shows no window touching it other than the desktop's own (the screen's panel and the one desktop window that spans the whole canvas, which are never closed) and those of a screen another member holds now (by its centre such a window is that member's own, never a leftover: a browser's frame reaches 16 px past its screen's edge, and it was closed when a colleague sat down beside it), or the computer is known absent or stopped. A returning member using a cached route cannot use that screen while cleanup is pending. A failed or cancelled transition attempts to restore the old holder to its exact screen, with its own last-use time (so the next attempt still takes the idle member's screen, not a busier one's), before unlocking; a failed restoration reports seat_restore_failed. Any resulting free screen still needs a fresh empty-screen proof before assignment, so no durable quarantine is required. All busy seats yield computer_busy. Status and previews never assign seats. Shared containers use 6 GiB, 4 CPUs and six screens, upgrading known one-screen shapes without removing home or team mounts. Member deletion holds seat_lock through disconnect and window cleanup before releasing seats on all hosts, rechecks the holder before each close, and erases only historical per-bot computers. Viewer opens and session entry points recheck the member, Target and current holder after awaits and revoke displaced sessions before authorizing access. Shared datasets use .raphael-data/members/<core member key>/ for databases, locks, imports and charts, with one common venv and install lock; the public schema, description and results stay unchanged, and the core adds the key only after checking the member's arguments. The runtime requires a string matching [0-9a-f]{16}, refuses symbolic member directories and refuses paths resolving outside .raphael-data/members/. Shared teaching accepts the member's own available seat and observes only the seat: its own window list as the wall projects it (one driver list_windows on the seat's connection) and its clipped RFB picture, through the core's input/tick; it has no relay observer and never calls a canvas-wide observation (get_accessibility_tree, get_desktop_state). Private titles, private-page marks of the seat, a peer's marked window touching the screen, a payment hold and any observation failure withhold text and pixels and record typed keys as private_input. An owner's press waits for that observation before it reaches the computer. Per-bot dataset paths and teaching calls remain unchanged. The core classifies every offered driver argument, including nested properties, in SHARED_ARGUMENTS and refuses unreviewed arguments, desktop coordinate frames, target, from_zoom, capture_id and screenshot_out_file in shared mode. Every named or bound native window is checked against fresh canvas rows and must fit wholly inside its seat before reads or input; a centre-owned straddling window is moved and resized first, otherwise refused. Native gesture points and the full interpolated drag stay inside the seat. Page target bindings are revoked when their native window leaves the seat, including private-picture and vault reads. In both modes, successful target/tab snapshots and active-tab binds remember the exact tab per computer target until disconnect. Every private-picture read and per-field vault read/type uses that target and tab; an unknown tab refuses filling and keeps private pages protected, without guessing or changing member-facing fill parameters. Window lists, placements, verify_state bounds and pointer prose use seat coordinates, while driver tree/token prose is preserved and page JSON appears once. run_steps requires a nonempty live-schema expect for a verify_state observation before any batch action; legacy predicates is a reply field, not an input alias. Desktop pictures come only from the seat's authenticated clipped RFB view. launch_app moves only the new windows tied to its launch (R-COMP-SEAT-02); every other window stays. The seat browser launcher selects now-minus-before windows with this launch's unique title and browser pid. The computer owns Chromium and its profile; per-seat driver sessions attach with existing_profile and never launch it. Displacement gently closes every window touching its screen except the desktop's own and confirms emptiness within a bounded time (LocalVm.seat_clean_seconds: every list and close is a command through the container boundary); an unconfirmed cleanup restores the exact old holder and yields computer_busy. Every free-screen allocation must pass the same check, skipping unconfirmed free screens and never cleaning an existing holder. Owner takeover checks only that seat's non-command activity and the unit native lock, while other seats' reads, page calls and commands continue. Layer 7 (the seat browser launcher and the existing-profile grant, on top of layer 6's picture, keys and X-server look) preserves known layer 3/4/5/6 mounts on rebuild. Clipboard, files and sign-ins are common; shell and browser still run as cua. In shared mode the shell has no screen: foreground and background vm_exec run without DISPLAY; open a window with launch_app. Members are the owner's own: deliberately restoring display access from the shell is outside the seat wall's accidental-input boundary. Not given and never claimed: the core's check and the driver's action are two requests to one X server, so a window that moves or grows by itself between them is not caught; what launch_app can and cannot tie is stated in R-COMP-SEAT-02. A private mark's way out can be lost and is then kept, never guessed: if a marked window is moved to another seat, its marker reads the old page target meanwhile (which drops that binding) and the tab is then closed, neither the closed-tab reply nor another site can be seen for that mark, and the marker's pictures (and those of a seat the window still touches) stay withheld until the core restarts. A class name is not an ownership boundary: a member that names its own window after the desktop's is outside the wall. A saved value is never typed into an embedded frame, so a form whose fields live in one (a payment provider's card frame) is the owner's to fill. Before a shared computer's first browser of each start, per-bot homes of this owner on this host that the record does not call handled are carried, inside Linux only (the computer's own image, --network none, the old home mounted read-only, ownership set to 1000:1000; the host's Python copies nothing). A home whose computer is busy is left for the next start; a running idle one is stopped, never removed. If the shared home has no browser profile, the profile of the home whose Default/Cookies is newest is carried first, without Singleton* locks and without the browser's caches; only one profile is ever carried. A member's files go to a folder named after the member (its key appended on a collision; an unknown existing folder is never merged), leaving out checkpoints.EXCLUDED, .raphael-data and the record; above 1 GiB of such files none is copied, but the home's sign-ins and datasets still are. Its top-level DuckDB files go to that member's dataset folder, never the venv. The core writes .raphael-carried.json in the shared home; handled or left-in-place homes never repeat, a failure is retried at the next start and after three failed copies the home is left in place. Every browser of the computer waits for the carry until the same moment, a budget below what browser_open allows, counted from the carry's start; the carry itself is not cut short and goes on behind the browser, keeping each old home's guard until that home's copy has ended. A helper container outlives its Docker CLI: one that runs out of its time is hung and is killed by its own name before the guard is released (when it cannot be seen to have ended or to be absent, its home stays closed to its old computer, which is refused as busy, for as long as that core lives); one whose core is closing is left to finish; one an earlier core left running holds its name, and the next core waits for it before asking that home again. What a copy publishes is handed to the computer's user while still staged in the helper's own folder, and folders made on the way at once, before any long copy; no path a member prepared is walked. A profile folder the browser has written to is kept; one its launcher has only just made, still empty, is replaced by the carried one. No failure keeps the browser from starting, and nothing is shown to the owner. Old homes, containers and volumes are never changed or removed. Stated limits: a home left in place stays reachable only on its old computer; when the home with the newest sign-ins is busy at that first start another home's profile is carried; members can write the record (they are the owner's own). The member folders of datasets keep members from colliding, not from each other: every member has the same workspace, user and venv by design, so a member that swaps its folder for a link while its own dataset call runs reaches what its shell reaches anyway. The helper is root only inside its own throwaway container, whose one writable mount is the workspace every member owns: a member that swaps a staging folder's name while a copy runs can make that carry fail, not reach anything else. A core that died while a helper ran leaves that home unguarded until the next core's carry reaches it. | pytest | hard | | R-COMP-SIZE-01 | 2026-11-15 | The shared computer was capped at 6 GiB and 4 CPUs and its CPU was throttled 48% of the time. | R-COMP-SEAT-02 | 2026-11-16 | A program a member launches at a seat opens on that member's screen, and no other member gets its window. Seen on a shared computer: a terminal launched at seat 5 stood on seat 1's screen and its member could not find it. The window manager opens a new window on the screen the pointer is on; the core moved a new window only when its pid was the launch's (or it was the one other new window) and looked once, but xfce4-terminal opens a later launch's window in the copy already running and the window often maps after that look. Each launch is tagged as a desktop launcher tags one: the driver gets `env DESKTOP_STARTUP_ID=<tag> RAPHAEL_LAUNCH=<tag> <program path> <the launch's other words>` with the additional arguments as given. The driver runs launch_path split on white space and without a shell (cua-driver 0.37.0 `spawn_launch_command`, though its definition says a shell): the first real-engine run sent `sh -c '<command>'`, the driver split the quote apart, nothing started, and it refused both seat launches (`launch_handoff_timeout`: its process ended with no new window within 8 s). Only a program the computer's shell finds (the first word of launch_path, else the name) is tagged, by its absolute path, so the driver's class match of a window handed to a copy already running reads the command as it would untagged; a desktop-entry name or URLs stay untagged. The driver's refusal stands, in the member's words: it reads every window on the canvas, so a window outside the seat never fails a launch. Its new windows are waited for up to 10 s under the unit native lock and judged once, in one command: the window's own `_NET_STARTUP_ID`, else its client leader's, decides, and another launch's id is never this one's whatever the process; with none, the tag in the process's environment or the launch's pid ties it. Tied windows are moved to the seat and named in the reply; an untied window is left where it opened, and when it stands off the seat the reply says so and how to start a separate copy (`xfce4-terminal --disable-server`, `browser_open` for pages); a launch with no window says so. The reply shows the member's own command, never the tag. Stated limits: a window opened by a program started with this launch's tag for an untagged request (a member's shell command, a desktop-entry launch) during the same 10 s is taken as this launch's; a window that maps after the wait stays where it opened; a program that neither writes the startup id nor runs in the launch's process cannot be tied. A seat's browser window (`browser_open`) is found by raphael-seat-browser's own title marker, not by this. | pytest, authorized container | hard | core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_launch_moves_only_the_windows_tied_to_it; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_launch_handed_to_the_copy_already_running_brings_its_window_to_the_members_screen; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_launch_that_runs_its_own_copy_brings_its_window_to_the_members_screen; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_window_that_maps_after_the_driver_answered_is_waited_for_and_brought; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_launch_that_opens_nothing_is_the_drivers_refusal_in_the_members_words; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_two_members_launching_at_once_each_get_their_own_window; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_window_nothing_ties_to_the_launch_stays_where_it_opened_and_the_reply_says_so; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_launch_with_no_window_says_so_after_its_bounded_wait; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_launch_is_tagged_so_that_its_windows_can_be_told_from_every_other_launchs; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_driver_reads_a_tagged_launch_as_it_reads_the_launch_untagged; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_window_is_a_launchs_by_its_startup_id_first_then_its_process; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_marks_command_reads_the_window_its_leader_and_its_process; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_computer_answers_where_a_name_is_a_program_and_what_ties_windows_in_one_command; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_shared_acceptance_drives_real_tools_through_every_numbered_check; `npm run verify -- --computer`: "shared launches land on their own screens"; features/member-computer.md |

| R-CARD-12 | 2026-11-11 | One question is open at a time in a conversation, and an ordinary message closes it (answering a newer card or sending a chat message closes older unanswered cards). What the owner writes in a conversation (typed in the app, or sent from a chat) or in a room's thread, while a question card waits there, is delivered as an ordinary message and the card closes, shown as closed and not as answered (R-CARD-08); the member that asked is told the question was left unanswered and what the owner said instead, and does not answer that message again. While a card waits, the composer of that conversation says 「輸入你的回答，或聊點別的」 in the screen language and returns to its usual words when nothing waits. ## Durable owner inbox

These additions refine R-BATCH-01 and R-UI-08 for private owner input; existing room routing remains unchanged.

| ID | Date | Rule | Proof | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-QUEUE-01 | 2026-11-03 | Private typed input is admitted once to a durable, uncapped FIFO per member and conversation. A claimed request waits for its answer; a busy request returns queued immediately. Only one claim runs per scope. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_inbox.py::test_cli_busy_input_is_admitted_immediately_and_answers_a_b_c_in_order; core/tests/agent/test_inbox.py; core/tests/agent/test_inbox_recovery.py |
| R-QUEUE-02 | 2026-11-03 | Read steer tickets settle once. Unread tickets return to the front of their FIFO however the work ends (R-QUEUE-10). Codex uses turn/steer. Claude Code and Antigravity take no input once started, so a message said meanwhile (no files, nothing queued before it) rides on the result of the turn's next call to Raphael's tools under one short label; it is read once, kept with the turn's records for a restart or a resumed session, and a message withdrawn before then is never read. A turn that calls no tool before it ends leaves it unread, and it starts the next turn in its FIFO place. The original answer is always published. This refines R-BATCH-01. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_inbox.py::test_unread_steer_survives_loop_end_as_the_next_turn; core/tests/agent/test_send_now.py::test_a_message_said_mid_turn_reaches_the_model_with_its_next_tool_result; core/tests/agent/test_send_now.py::test_a_message_the_turn_never_read_starts_the_next_turn_once; core/tests/agent/test_send_now.py::test_a_message_withdrawn_while_steering_is_never_read; core/tests/agent/test_cli_steering.py::test_claude_and_antigravity_take_the_owner_words_at_their_next_step_and_codex_keeps_turn_steer; core/tests/agent/test_steering.py; core/tests/agent/test_cli_steering.py; core/tests/agent/test_inbox.py |

## Meetings and private work

This owner decision supersedes the sequential room speech and room-scope tool-work clauses above.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-ROOM-01 | 2026-11-04 | Speech offers only handoff, room_post, ask_member and read-only work_status. Self-handoff creates ordinary private work with a separate room report destination. No approval card is projected into the meeting. | pytest | hard | core/tests/background/test_room_meeting.py::test_self_lane_reads_private_context_and_reports_a_short_card_once; core/tests/background/test_room_cli.py::test_cli_speech_never_opens_an_approval_or_calls_a_plugin |
| R-ROOM-02 | 2026-11-10 | Room speech shares two slots, independently of three private work slots and per-member work admission. No model call asks a member beforehand whether it will speak: an address goes to whoever it names; a message that names no one goes to the room's default responder first, and every other member then reads that answer in its own speech turn and speaks or passes. A member the owner addresses may not pass; its pass is the status line no_reply. Ordinary room rounds use one seen-through snapshot. Explicit meetings give every participant a turn in order, even after earlier passes; owner corrections wake all participants again. A reply has no total clock: each attempt has the whole no-progress window (ROOM_STALL_S), a chosen wait before the next attempt is no stall, and an attempt that hears nothing for the window ends the reply as stalled. A reply that does not finish is a status line only: no bubble in its member's name, no close-out in the chief's, and the provider's own words only in private records and logs. Meeting status comes from persisted exchange state. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/members/test_room_turns.py; core/tests/background/test_room_attempts.py; core/tests/background/test_room_meeting.py::test_all_speech_slots_run_while_the_same_member_has_private_work; core/tests/members/test_meetings_followup.py; app/src/main/group-chats.test.ts; room-concurrent-replies; room-reply-status |
| R-ROOM-03 | 2026-11-04 | Prompts carry a computed per-member board, pinned chief decisions and messages after the member's successful reading cursor. No stage has two work owners. A changed model starts its own session and never reads another's. A session is given up at 80% of the window and the renewed one is given the thread's rolling summary (decisions and progress) before the newest whole messages that fit. | R-UI-121 | 2026-11-11 | A press outside an open menu, popover, picker or card closes it and is delivered to what was pressed, in the same press, as if nothing had been open; it is never spent on closing. A dialog with a scrim is the exception below. A menu or popover lets the press through untouched (its own pointerdown, focus and click), and a drawer's scrim (the narrow sidebar, the details panel) closes its panel and then gives the element under the pointer the focus and one click, once. A dialog's scrim (Connect apps, Settings, the member creator, the model chooser, the delete question) only closes it and the press is spent: nothing behind it is pressed, focused or right-clicked, and no selection starts. A menu open over a dialog is closed first and the dialog by the next press, since the scrim hides the page until then. Presses that began inside the panel, a selection dragged out of it, and a press the panel may not close right now (a busy dialog, a calendar editor holding typed words) close nothing and press nothing. A right click outside a menu or popover closes it and opens the menu of what it pressed, if that has one; over a dialog's scrim it only closes the dialog; an 'are you sure' step is abandoned by any outside press, which still goes through. The focus follows the pressed element; Escape and the keyboard are unchanged. One policy in dismissals.ts serves every dialog, menu and popover. The one control that is not pressed is the one that opened the thing, which only closes it (R-UI-124). A frame (a page open in the conversation's place, a computer's screen) is another document, so a press inside it gives the app no pointer event: the app takes the frame receiving the focus with the pointer over it for that press, and closes every panel the frame is outside of while the frame keeps the press. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/dismissals.test.ts::a press on a control outside an open popover closes it and the control's handler runs once, with the focus on it; | R-UI-124 | 2026-11-11 | The control that opened a menu, popover, picker, card or dialog is its toggle. A press on it while the thing is open closes it and does nothing else, and it stays closed: nothing opens it again, whether the press lands on the control itself or on the scrim over it (a dialog's scrim hands nothing down, R-UI-121), on the anchor of a menu, or is Enter or Space on the focused control (Space closes at once, so that its release cannot open it again). Outside a menu or popover every other control is still pressed as R-UI-121 says, behind a dialog's scrim none is, and Escape is unchanged. One mechanism in dismissals.ts does it for every opener: a panel records the control the last click activated when it registers (a menu item that opens a dialog stands for the menu's own opener; a list drawn again is matched by its key), and a drawer's scrim takes its opener from its caller. A field that takes typing is never an opener. A right click opens a row's menu from the row's ⋯ and the row is not its opener: a left press on that row closes the menu and opens the row (R-UI-121), and a second right click on it takes the menu to the new point. | R-REACT-01 | 2026-11-14 | A message carries at most one reaction of the owner's, where it is stored: the primary key is the message (migration 82 kept the newest of a message that had several), and choosing another emoji replaces it in one write. Every reaction is analysed, at once: any reaction of the owner's to a reply of a member's own, positive or negative (Raphael too; in a group room only the reacted message's author), starts that member's own turn at once, with no wait and no list of emoji that count: `source` reaction, empty question, started by an internal note that names the emoji and holds the reply, the owner's message and the few turns before as data, on the member's own model with no tool, no other model to fall back on and no message of the owner's. It shows at once as the member's usual presence in the conversation (「思考中」). The member decides, in its own voice and in the owner's language (the window's language is named in the prompt and wins over what the member teaches or the conversation holds, unless the owner's own last words are in another; the reaction prompt carries no rule that lets a persona choose, and the task comes after the persona; the rule for an ordinary reply, `REPLY_LANGUAGE`, no longer follows an "established conversation": it answers in the language of the owner's latest message, not a lesson's): ask in one or two sentences what was wrong when the emoji shows dissatisfaction (to say quickly what they are unhappy with), say a few words (a brief acknowledgement, the obvious next step), or say nothing, and then the turn is taken out of the thread: no message, card, failure or notice shows that it ran. Nothing is a pass: the member answers exactly (pass), as a member does in a group room that has nothing to add (`is_pass`: any case, brackets, a full stop or bold). The answer format has no field for silence, and the loop sends an empty answer back for words, so the room's own pass is reused rather than a new token. Cost: every reaction is one model call on that member's model (accepted by the owner). A reaction taken back, or changed to another emoji, while its turn runs ends that turn and leaves nothing (a changed one starts the new emoji's), and so does the owner writing in that conversation (their message is not held up). A reaction is analysed once per message and emoji (`reaction_asks`, keyed by kind, message and emoji since migration 83): the same emoji given again is not analysed again, another emoji is; one that was ended or failed was never made, so it is analysed when it is given again. A member working for the owner in that conversation starts none, and the reaction is then not recorded: it is skipped, not queued, because the owner's turn already shows the member at work and an answer to a reaction would come after it, out of its place (analyses of reactions wait on each other, and never count as work for the owner). Never analysed: the owner's own message, the member's own answer to a reaction, a quick conversation, a reply that did not finish. In a group room only the reacted message's own author thinks, in the same thread, through the room's own speech turn (no other member, handoff or @), and not while the thread is being discussed; what it makes is no discussion of the room (not its meeting, not what it is busy with, and Stop wraps nothing up for it). A reaction is metadata, never approval and never the owner's words: no card, draft or question is settled by it, and the analysis is neither a note for the memory nor an instruction. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/agent/test_emoji_reactions.py::test_a_message_carries_one_reaction_choosing_another_replaces_it_and_the_same_again_takes_it_back; | R-CLI-10 | 2026-11-11 | Length is not an error for a fresh native session. The mandatory part (this turn's request, attachment names, additions, note and records) is never cut and raises `context_overflow` only when it alone outgrows the window or a provider byte limit. What is left is filled in this order: memory and team sections, the conversation summary, the owner's own recent words (newest first, capped), then recent turns newest first, whole. ## Page text remains a draft

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-CARD-11 | 2026-11-05 | A model/MCP page never speaks as the owner. ## Approved computer and storage hardening

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SEC-SSH-01 | 2026-11-05 | Remote Docker uses a private Unix socket under an owner-only directory, never an unauthenticated TCP or SOCKS forward. Unsupported SSH/Docker clients fall back to per-command ssh:// transport. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_followup_security.py; core/tests/computer/test_smooth_computer.py::test_ten_remote_docker_calls_share_one_ssh_connection |
| R-SEC-PRIVATE-01 | 2026-11-05 | Filled pages have no screenshot expiry. Each mark belongs to one exact computer/page target/tab and retains its physical computer and original bound native window. On a shared computer, it also withholds another seat's pictures whenever that window overlaps the seat, or the required fresh window list fails; an absent window affects only the marker's seat. Both the pre-call and post-call decisions apply (before the call only for computer tools; after it for anything that returns a picture), each from one window-geometry command, and nothing is listed when there are no peer marks. A complete semantic read of that same tab showing a different HTTP(S) origin with none of the filled refs lifts protection. The private-picture probe also retires that exact tab's mark on the driver's refusal that this tab is gone, in either form it gives (both measured on 0.37.0 and each matched whole): for a browser the seat attached to, an error whose message is "confirmation provider failed: tab <this tab id> no longer has a live CDP page target"; for a browser the driver launched itself (per-bot), a refusal that is NOT an error, code browser_tab_not_found, message "cannot attach to tab target <CDP id>: CDP Target.attachToTarget failed (-32602): No target with given id found". A tab id the driver never knew has the same codes and other words, and keeps the mark. Same-origin navigation, lost fields, incomplete reads, every other error, timeout, disconnect and other-tab reads or binds retain protection. Reads return a value-free semantic tree and explanation. A safe observation only authorizes subsequent captures, never pixels captured before it. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_followup_security.py::test_private_page_requires_changed_origin_and_absent_fields; core/tests/computer/test_followup_security.py::test_navigation_after_capture_cannot_release_the_captured_private_picture; core/tests/computer/test_login_visibility.py |
| R-SEC-LOG-02 | 2026-11-05 | Rust caps native stderr lines at 16 KiB, discards overlong lines in full, and applies Python-equivalent credential scrubbing before the log sink. Invalid UTF-8 and EOF are handled without exposing partial credentials. | cargo | hard | app/src-tauri/src/core_stderr.rs |
| R-SEC-DATA-01 | 2026-11-05 | Before data/memory directory use, Windows applies a protected owner-SID-only inheritable DACL; POSIX uses mode 0700. ACL failure stops use. Migration backups retain the newest five versions per source and delete only unchanged files recorded as app-created; untracked or owner-edited files remain. | pytest | hard | core/tests/test_storage_security.py; core/tests/memory/test_files.py |

By default it states when its data is from and that it only states recorded facts (the promise stays the default); a member may give `summary`, one line of at most 160 characters, which takes that line's place (R-PAGE-07); findings carry status words in the theme's colours, a table has tabs for its groups, and the team is boxes in each member's colour joined by labelled right-angle arrows that cover nothing. The read tool `team_work` returns recorded facts only, in the owner's private chat only. The report's one button is 存成檔案. Nothing floats over the page: the button stands where a thread's share stands, first of the conversation header's tools at the top right, the same 36 px round button, drawn as a file with an arrow into it and never as the arrow out of a box that shares a member. That one place holds what is on screen: on a thread the member's share, whose card is one row with a mark drawn for it (the member's own face twice, the copy behind and above the one that is, no stock icon), on a report its 存成檔案, and on an HTML page (R-PAGE-08) the same button (a plugin's page, which works only through its host, has none). A press saves the report as one HTML file of the app's own drawing in the owner's output folder, or Downloads when none is chosen or it takes nothing, never over another file, selects it in File Explorer and says where it went, and the report stays open. An HTML page is saved the same way as one file that the core builds of the page it keeps (`pages.export`: the window gives its id and no text), without the helper that talks to the window, in its reader's own theme and under the page's own policy in its head (its sources, no connection, no form, no frame), after the same secret scan. Nothing in the file runs or loads: its policy stands first, the core writes it only for a report page and only with that drawing's markup, and no folder comes from the window. It follows its reader's light or dark theme, white on paper, and its team is drawn for the width it is read at. | R-PAGE-14 | 2026-11-19 | What the owner did on a page was gone after 返回 (issue 236): on a Mac the frame reloads whenever it moves (WebKit has no `moveBefore`), every platform reloads it on a theme change, a new revision or leaving the conversation, and the sandboxed frame has no storage of its own. Now what the owner does on a page stays with it: the page's helper (core agent/pages.py) sends every field's value (inputs, text areas, selects, editable text), what the page keeps in localStorage (a stand-in the helper gives the sandbox) and what its script keeps with `window.raphael.state.set(value)` to the window, 400 ms after the last change; the window gives it to the core for the page that frame shows (`pages.state`), which keeps it with the page (table `page_state`, gone with the page), at most 64 KB and refused when it holds a credential. When the page opens again, after a reload, a theme change, a new revision or a restart, the core serves it back with the page and the helper puts every field back before the owner sees it, as the owner's own change the page's script hears (it does not send or open anything meanwhile), and gives the storage and `raphael.state.get()` back. A password, a file, a hidden field and one marked `autocomplete="off"` or `data-raphael-forget` are never kept; the file a page is saved as never carries the state. Members need no word of it (their prompt is unchanged): the fields and localStorage work as in any browser, and `raphael.state` is there for a script that wants it. | pytest, vitest, cargo test, look | hard | core/tests/agent/test_pages.py::test_what_the_owner_left_on_a_page_is_served_back_with_it_and_never_in_its_file; core/tests/agent/test_pages.py::test_a_state_that_is_not_fields_storage_and_a_value_or_holds_a_secret_is_refused; core/tests/agent/test_pages.py::test_the_state_goes_with_its_page_and_a_later_save_replaces_it; core/tests/agent/test_pages.py::test_the_helper_keeps_fields_storage_and_the_scripts_value_and_never_a_password; app/src/main/page-card.test.ts::what the owner left on the page goes to the core for that page, and nothing else does (R-PAGE-14); app/src-tauri/src/commands.rs::a_report_is_saved_through_its_own_command_alone; page-state-kept; page-state-restored |

| R-PAGE-07 | 2026-11-14 | A report (`report` of show_page, drawn natively with every word escaped, R-PAGE-01) reads as a document. `summary` (optional, one line, at most 160 characters, read for secrets) replaces the default line under the title and is drawn at the text's size and colour; the date and the promise of recorded facts are the default, kept whenever no summary is given. The contents are a box, 「本頁內容」 over a list of the titled sections (a column of its own beside a page 960 px wide, as before; more than six sections still list only there). A `callout` section (`tone` info, warn or ok, an optional `heading`, `text` of at most 600 characters) is a note in the tone's colour on a tint of it with the tone's own icon. A table cell is text or a link `{text, url}`: the address is https only, with a host, no password, no space, quote or bracket, at most 2048 characters, and read by the same secret scanner as the page (R-PAGE-05, R-SECRET-01), so a map link with a percent-encoded name or a news address with an article number passes and a credential inside a link (`?key=AIza…`, `?token=…`) does not; the app draws it in the theme's blue, underlined, and a press opens it in the owner's own browser through `open_external_url`, never in the window (its default is stopped). A table's `filter` names one column whose values (at most 24 different; the checker's refusal says which column and how many) become a dropdown over the table, the column's name small above it and 全部 first, drawn only where there are two values; it works with the group tabs, and when the two leave no row the table says 沒有符合的項目 in a row. A column whose cells with words are all figures is set at the right, head and cells, and a column of short words (at most 12 characters, links among them) keeps to one line so a long column beside it cannot squeeze it. Limits are 100 rows and 160 characters a cell. A report's title is 1.45 times the 22 px heading size, 31.9 px (1.2 times, 26.4 px, under 640 px; the type scale has no size of its own for it) and its section titles 22 px (18 px). In the saved file (R-PAGE-01) nothing runs, so a file has no dropdown and no tabs, but its link cells are links: the core accepts `href` within the page or to an https address and the attribute `rel`, and nothing else (a plain http, a script or a password in an address is refused). The tool tells members the new fields in about 25 words; everything else is the checker's refusal. ## Subscription quota warning

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-QUOTA-01 | 2026-11-10 | When a subscription window (Codex, Claude, Antigravity; 5 hours or weekly) has 10% or less left, the owner is told once per provider, period and reset time, whoever read it (a CLI turn, a background task or the usage page). The main window shows one toast with the subscription, the window, what is left and when it resets; a hidden window shows it when it is next seen, and not at all once that window has reset. A spent window (its own notice says so), a report of a window already over, a subscription blocked by another window and a reading no one hears tell nothing. The `quota_warned` setting keeps what was told, so a restart says nothing again and the next window after a reset is told again; a told window read again with 20% or more left (a voucher, a reset that kept its end) is told again when it falls to the last tenth.
