## Where a slime moves, and a screen that never flickers

A screen never flickers or jumps when a caption or other text updates. This section supersedes every place in R-UI-74, R-UI-99 and R-UI-78 that lets a slime move in the header, the panel, a card, a group, a dialog or the guide.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SLIME-01 | 2026-11-11 | A slime moves in exactly two places: a member's row (or pinned tile) in the left member list, while that member works, and the thinking or waiting line of the open conversation (a private thread or a group). Everywhere else it is a picture of that slime: the conversation header, the details panel, the member settings page, group faces and their header cluster, hand-off and other cards, the new-member maker and the slime picker, mentions, dialogs, the guide's slimes, the computer's boot face and the search. A picture keeps its colour, hat, crown and accessories and the pose of rest (idle, or sleeping after a day's silence); it has no CSS animation, no transition, no strips, no clock and no state to hand over, and nothing observes it. `faceHTML` and `slimeFaceHTML` draw a picture unless they are given `moving`, and only the member row, the pinned tile and the thinking line give it. At a compact width the list is a drawer: while it is closed no slime is drawn there, so the look check asks for a moving one in the list only while the list is on screen. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a slime is a picture unless it moves: whatever its member does, it keeps its resting pose and no clock; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a picture keeps the states that are poses without motion, asleep and at rest, and a wait for the owner is only a slime at rest; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::faceHTML moves only when asked, for Raphael and for a member alike; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::the pictures of the picker, the maker and the stores never move; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::slime.css holds a picture still against every rule that could move it; app/src/main/ui-patch.test.ts::a picture of a slime is kept as it is by a redraw, and another member takes a fresh one; app/src/main/slime-offscreen.test.ts::a picture of a slime is never watched, so it costs the page nothing; app/scripts/look/slime-places.test.mjs::a slime in the member list or the thinking line may move, and one anywhere else must be a picture; app/scripts/look/slime-places.test.mjs::a list that is not on screen is not asked to move, and one that is on screen is; slime-places; slime-places-settings |
| R-SLIME-02 | 2026-11-11 | An update from the core touches only what changed. A member's step from one activity to another, or new words for what it is doing, change neither the identity of a thread line nor its face: a moving face is left out of a line's identity and the face already on screen is handed over by `setSlimeState` and `followSlimeRoster`, so the turn is not replaced, its words are not drawn again and nothing restarts. The conversations in the panel, the chip of the model and the model list are patched or left alone, never written anew, and the panel and the settings page do not draw again for a member's last line, time, unread mark or activity, nor does such a roster start a read of the panel's Library; a member's skills and commands are read when the slash menu is wanted, not at every roster event. The strips of a moving face (some thousands of nodes for a thinking or searching one) go in a slice of about 200 nodes a frame, out of sight, and show together, so that a change of state never holds a frame for 50 ms. A read that fails keeps what is on screen. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/thread-diff.test.ts::a line keeps its identity when only the state or the words of a member's face change; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a roster read hands a face on screen over before the redraw, so the redraw keeps it; app/src/main/conversation-list.test.ts::a refresh patches the rows already on screen and a failed read keeps them; app/src/main/members.test.ts::a page about a member does not compare what changes with each word it says; app/src/main/pages/agent-members.test.ts::a roster that moves only a last line, a step or an unread mark starts no Library read and leaves the panel alone, and a renamed member reads and shows; app/src/main/slash-menu.test.ts::a member change reads nothing for a closed menu, and the next slash reads the skills it may have added; app/src/main/slime.test.ts::a heavy state's strips go in a slice a frame, out of sight, and show together, and a newer state drops the rest; app/scripts/look/event-storm.test.mjs::a storm that replaces a node, empties text, moves a scroll or animates a picture is told apart from a calm one; busy-team-calm |
| R-SLIME-03 | 2026-11-14 | A reply that queued research in the background once showed an empty slime under it and, under that, a task line with the slime and its progress words: two slimes. One member shows at most one working slime in one conversation, whatever the number of turns, tasks, cards and messages on their way. `standingPresence` (timeline.ts) is the one rule: of the turns that want a slime (`presenceWant`: `words` while a turn or its task works or its replies are being read in, `bare` while it waits for the owner at a card, a question, a yes or a waiting task, nothing once it is over) the newest one that works stands, so a background task's line with its progress words wins over an empty slime; a face that only waits stands only when nothing works, and then the newest of those. Every other turn draws none (`TurnLook.presenceTurnId`). What the owner has just sent or is saying (a pending bubble, a voice message) is newer than every turn and takes the slime itself: the line under the newest of them, carrying the working turn's words, and no turn draws its own beside it. A line's cached drawing knows whether it holds the slime, so a line that gives it up is drawn again. A group's thinking lines are per member and per message, not a thread (R-AG-02). | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/standing-presence.test.ts::a turn wants words while it works, a bare face while it waits, and nothing once it is over; app/src/main/standing-presence.test.ts::two tasks: the newest stands, the older line draws none; app/src/main/standing-presence.test.ts::a task with words stands before a newer face that only waits; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a reply that handed work to the background shows one slime, on the task line; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a turn that is running without a task shows one slime; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::two background tasks of one member show one slime, on the newest; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a task line with words wins over a face that only waits, as in the owner's picture; app/src/main/pages/agent-stop.test.ts::a message just sent takes the one slime from a task that waits; slime-one-working |

## A model's link to a file it delivered

A routine's message ended with the words `[已查核的部分紀錄](attachment:4e43d6d3fd9d47fab8b552f849c3c48b.md)` shown as they were written.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-MSG-LINK-01 | 2026-11-14 | A model writes a markdown link to a file it delivered by its stored id, `[words](attachment:<id>)` (the core reads the same form, and also `attachment:attachments/<id>` and `attachments/<id>`). The message renderer never shows that target. When the id is one of the conversation's own files (what the owner attached to a turn, and what a member delivered in a media message of any turn of that conversation: `knownFiles`) the link is that file's chip (`fileChipHTML`, the chip of a file in the team folder) with the link's words, or the file's name when it has none, and pressing it opens that file by `data-act="attachment-open"` as every other file of a message opens (`attachments.path`, by id only). Any other target of the `attachment:` kind (an id no turn holds, a path, a drive letter, a malformed or upper-case id) is only its words, with no button, no link and no `data-id`. Only the stored-id form `^[a-f0-9]{32}\.[a-z0-9]{1,12}$` is ever read, so no path reaches `open_attachment`. A renderer that is not given the conversation's files (a group, search, copy) shows the words alone. A reply drawn before its file was known is drawn again once it is (the kept drawing and a turn's cache key carry the known ids its words mention). | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::only the stored ids of the two link forms are read; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a link to a file of the conversation is its chip, opened by its id; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a link to a file the conversation does not know is its words alone; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::where nothing is known, the words stand alone and no raw target shows; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a routine's message links the file it delivered in the same turn; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a file of an earlier turn of the conversation opens from a later reply, and one of no turn does not; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a reply that was drawn before the file was known is drawn again once it is; app/src/main/pages/agent-attachment-link.test.ts::the link is the file's chip, which opens that file once; a file of no turn is its words alone; message-attachment-link |
| R-MSG-LINK-02 | 2026-11-16 | A model also links a picture it delivered as an image, `![words](attachment:<id>)`, and the message renderer once showed that markdown raw in the reply under the picture. It never shows. An image link to one of the conversation's own files (`knownFiles`, R-MSG-LINK-01) is the same chip as a plain link to that file, with the link's words or the file's name, and opens it by its id; the picture itself stays the message it was sent in (R-UI-156) and is not drawn a second time. An image link to a file the conversation does not have, or to any other `attachment:` target (a path, a malformed id), says in words that the picture cannot be shown (「圖片「<words>」無法顯示」, or 「這張圖片無法顯示」 with no words), with no button, link, picture or id. A renderer that is not given the conversation's files (a group, search, copy) shows the words alone. An image link still arriving shows its words, never the part of its target that has come. A page cannot reach the app's stored files (it runs on its own host with no fetch, R-PAGE-13), and nothing puts them into a page. | vitest | hard | app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a picture linked by its stored id is the chip of the conversation's file, never its markdown; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a picture the conversation does not have says so in words, with no button and no raw target; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a picture link still arriving shows its words, never the part of its target that has come; app/src/main/attachment-links.test.ts::a reply that links the picture its turn just delivered shows the picture once and the link as its chip |
| R-MSG-LINK-03 | 2026-11-17 | Members sent the owner on Telegram links he could not open on his phone (in his database: three replies naming a file by its path in the member's computer). No message to an outside chat (Telegram, Slack or Discord; Raphael's bot or a member's own; a reply, a task's report, a routine's) carries a link the owner's phone cannot open. Every one leaves through `Switchboard.tell`, which passes it through `messaging/links.py deliverable`; the one judgement is `unreachable`: a path of a computer (POSIX under a system top folder, `~/`, a drive letter, a network share, `file:`, `sandbox:`), an `attachment:` link, a page of this PC's page host and any other address of this PC, of a member's computer or of a private network (loopback, private, link-local and shared ranges, a name of one label or under a local top-level domain, `localhost:<port>` without a scheme). A link that names a file the core finds goes as that file through the channel's own upload, after the messages and once (not again when the turn already delivered it), and its words become the file's name: a file of the member's `/home/cua/workspace` or `/home/cua/team` read from the folder this PC keeps for it (inside it once every link is followed, at most 50 MiB, at most ten files and 100 MiB a delivery; none for a computer on another host), a stored `attachment:` id, or a page as the HTML file the app saves of it (R-PAGE-08; a report or a plugin's page is none). Anything else is replaced by its name or words and a note in the interface language (that the file could not be sent and opens on the computer, or that the link opens only on the computer), never left as the link. A public link (and `mailto:`, `tg:`, an API path such as `/api/users`) is never touched; a link in a fenced code block is code; nothing reads the words around a link, so it holds in every language. A path of this PC is never uploaded for a link (R-BATCH-02). The member is told: the turn it answers from a chat says with its source line that the phone opens public links only and that a file goes as the file (`attach_file`), and a turn of the conversation so far that went over a chat says how many of its links or paths a phone cannot open. | pytest | hard | core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_what_a_phone_cannot_open_is_told_apart; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_a_public_link_and_what_is_no_link_are_left_alone; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_links_are_found_where_they_stand_whatever_the_script_around_them; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_a_file_found_goes_as_the_file_and_its_link_becomes_its_name; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_what_is_not_found_is_said_in_plain_words_and_no_dead_link_is_left; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_a_file_already_going_with_the_reply_is_not_sent_twice_and_a_stored_file_takes_its_words; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_the_member_computer_folders_the_store_and_a_page_are_where_files_are_found; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_the_switchboard_sends_the_file_a_reply_names_and_asks_in_the_writers_computer; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_a_reply_and_a_report_over_telegram_carry_the_file_and_no_dead_link; core/tests/messaging/test_links.py::test_the_app_finds_a_members_file_a_stored_file_and_a_page_and_names_the_member_of_a_report; core/tests/agent/test_card_context.py::test_incoming_platform_is_visible_in_actual_next_prompt; core/tests/agent/test_card_context.py::test_a_turn_that_went_over_a_chat_says_how_many_of_its_links_a_phone_could_not_open; features/conversation.md |

## First start-up

Logs of the first launch of a build showed the core ready 7 s after launch (14 s after a night of idle) against 3 to 4 s on a warm disk, and the first view's data 13 to 15 s after launch because the optional reads arrived with it.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-START-01 | 2026-11-11 | The core imports its speech libraries (numpy, sherpa-onnx, PortAudio) on a thread of its own that starts before the application module is imported, so the two overlap; the thread is joined before the first read of stdin, which keeps the rule that numpy loads before the stdin reader exists. Nothing native is imported before `claim_stdout`. | pytest | hard | core/tests/test_main_startup.py::test_speech_libraries_overlap_the_application_import_and_finish_before_stdin |
| R-START-02 | 2026-11-11 | At start-up the window asks the core for the first view (the roster, the rooms, the open conversation) first. Optional reads (`models.catalog`, `brains.cli`, the Connect apps lists, the Settings read-ahead) wait until the first page has been asked for and the core has answered everything the window asked and been quiet for 200 ms, then run one at a time; the time since launch plays no part. A core that never answers does not hold them for more than 60 s. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/startup-reads.test.ts::optional reads wait for the first view and for the core to go quiet, then run one at a time; app/scripts/look/driver.test.mjs::rejects an optional read that begins while the first view is still waiting (the burst that slowed Home to 4 s); look scene start-startup-order |
| R-START-03 | 2026-11-11 | While the window starts up, a shared read answered less than 1.5 s ago is the answer to the same read again, unless a change was asked of the core or an event other than loudness was heard since it was asked. The allowances are the exception: a reading under way is joined, but one answered a moment ago never answers the next (R-UI-109). After start-up every read goes to the core. | vitest | hard | app/src/main/api-startup-reads.test.ts::while the window starts up, a roster answered a moment ago answers the same read again; app/src/main/api-startup-reads.test.ts::a change made since, or an event heard since the read was asked, makes it a new read |
| R-START-04 | 2026-11-11 | `connectors.catalog` keeps its last good list in `connectors-catalog.json` in the data folder (written whole, no secret in it) and answers from it at once in a new run; a list older than an hour is renewed after the answer and `connectors.changed` is sent only if it changed. A failed renewal keeps the list and the next ask reports it as `stale`; with no list on disk the old paths (including `missing_key`) stay. A new key removes the file. | pytest | hard | core/tests/test_connectors.py::test_store_catalog_survives_a_restart_on_disk_and_answers_before_any_request; core/tests/test_connectors.py::test_an_old_saved_store_catalog_answers_at_once_and_is_renewed_after_it; core/tests/test_connectors.py::test_an_old_saved_store_catalog_that_cannot_be_renewed_says_so_on_the_next_ask |
| R-START-05 | 2026-11-11 | `models.catalog` answers with the providers' saved model lists and renews them after the answer; `providers.changed` tells the window when the renewal changed them. A provider with no saved list yet is waited for, as before. | pytest | hard | core/tests/brain/test_providers.py::test_models_catalog_answers_from_the_saved_lists_and_renews_them_after_the_answer |
| R-START-06 | 2026-11-11 | `members.list` and every member view carry no picture: a picture is named by `avatar_rev` (and `cover.rev`), a hash of its bytes, and read once through `members.picture {id, kind}` -> `{rev, url}`. The window keeps pictures by revision (`member-pictures.ts`), fills `avatar_url` and `cover.url` before a members call returns, and reads a picture again only when its revision changes. | R-START-07 | 2026-11-18 | Every state a page may ask for from its first moment is managed on the builder before any window exists (`early_state` in `lib.rs`), never in `setup` after a window is made: a page that asks first makes `state()` panic and the release build abort. The 2.0.0 installer check found it after an upgrade: on a warm webview the sidebar's update notice asked `update_control` before `setup` reached `updates::start`, and the app ended 1.3 s after launch (the device settings had the same race once, `load_device_settings`). ## Skill learning, and the data-floor refusal

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SKILL-09 | 2026-11-11 | A member's skill review is driven by a count: it counts the steps of real work (tool, command, desktop; not reads of skills, memory, the time or the team, and not chat) since the last review or since `skill_manage` ran, per member, and is due once the count reaches `NUDGE_INTERVAL` = 10. It is decided at the end of a turn that finished normally and is the owner's: never for a routine's run, an exchange between members, or a turn that was stopped, failed or is still running. A review restarts the count when it starts, whatever comes of it. ## A card says why it did not connect, and the owner's own Google

Connecting Gmail failed twice without a named cause. Once the card said `not_configured` (no key then); once it ended in a bare 「無法連線」 although no Composio request had been sent, and the member could only say "press again".

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-CONNECT-01 | 2026-11-11 | Every way a connect attempt can fail is a named cause (`CAUSES` in `connectors/cards.py`), never the bare 「沒有連上」: one sentence in the window's language that says what happened and the one thing to do (check the key, sign in again, the service is down so try later, this PC is offline, the address is wrong, the service refused with its own short words), on the card with a retry only where pressing again can help (`retry`) and the place to put something right where it cannot (`fix`: the Composio key in Connections, or the card's own form). The attempt that used to be bare had failed before any request was sent: another sign-in or the connection list still held the connector (`busy`), the vault could not be read (`vault`), the browser could not be opened (`browser`). The member is told the same cause as data (`cause <name>: <what happened>`), so that it explains instead of saying "press again". What the code cannot name is `unknown`, said honestly, with its short technical reason (a word and a status, never JSON or a trace) folded under 詳細資料. ## A subscription CLI that cannot take a turn

A member whose Claude Code sign-in had expired replied with one sentence for two causes (「還沒登入或缺少金鑰」) pointing at a settings page, with nothing to press, and the owner had to run `/login` in Claude by hand.

Each is told apart from the others, from an API brain's key (a refused key is the provider's `開啟設定`, never "sign in") and from every other failure, which keeps its own kind and no cause. The sentence carries no code, no setting path, no raw output and no second cause (R-TEXT-01..04), and a refused session is not asked again with the same session. The cause is the turn's public failure (`cause`, only for `cli:` providers) and a room or routine run records it (`cli_login_expired`, `cli_login`, `cli_missing`, in the room's words 「CLI 的登入已過期」, 「需要登入 CLI」, 「CLI 還沒安裝」). | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_a_session_that_ran_out_is_expired; core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_a_cli_that_says_it_is_not_logged_in_is_signed_out; core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_any_other_failure_stays_what_it_was_and_has_no_cause; core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_the_sentence_names_the_tool_and_one_cause_in_every_language; core/tests/agent/test_cli_signin_cause.py::test_a_cause_reaches_the_turn_only_from_a_subscription_cli_and_without_the_reason; core/tests/members/test_cli_brains.py::test_an_expired_sign_in_is_said_as_expired_and_is_not_asked_again; core/tests/members/test_cli_brains.py::test_a_cli_that_is_not_installed_says_so_and_never_starts; core/tests/members/test_cli_brains.py::test_not_logged_in_never_starts_the_fake_cli; core/tests/background/test_room_cli.py::test_a_room_member_whose_cli_cannot_sign_in_reports_that_cause; core/tests/background/test_room_cli.py::test_a_routine_run_whose_cli_sign_in_ran_out_is_a_failed_run_of_that_cause; core/tests/agent/test_service.py::test_a_key_openrouter_refuses_is_still_auth_after_the_harness_attempt |

## One row at the top of a conversation

The top of a conversation is one row, not two, and the thread passes under that row.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-TOP-01 | 2026-11-12 | A conversation, a member's and a group's, has one row above its thread, and it is the window's own top row: the header's pill and its round buttons are centred 30 px from the window's top, with no empty strip above them. The tools at its right end 14 px short of the window's three buttons where the pane reaches the window's edge; where another pane stands at the right (the details panel, a group's side thread) that pane's own head is the row over it, carries the window's buttons, and the conversation's tools go to their pane's edge. On a Mac, where macOS draws its traffic lights at the top left, the right end keeps no room for buttons: the tools keep the pane's own inset, at least 16 px clear of the window's edge, and the details panel's last button ends its padding (16 px) short of the edge, as its first starts at the left. At narrow widths the sidebar is the rail of faces (R-SIDE-01), so the row starts at the pane's own edge: no menu button stands at the window's top left. The thread passes under the row: its first message keeps a top padding equal to the row, and what scrolls up fades out as it reaches the row (the thread's mask, a report's, nothing new), while the pill and the buttons stay on their own opaque surfaces. The row's empty parts still move the window and maximise it, and a wheel over the row still moves the thread; the pill and the buttons still press. The search bar, a page or a report open in the thread's place, and the conversation between members sit under or in place of the row without moving it. Every other page keeps its place under the window's strip. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the row is 60 px, so the pill's centre stands 30 px from the window's top, and the buttons' cells are the width the tools stay clear of; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::on a Mac the row's right end keeps no room for caption buttons, which macOS draws at the top left, only the panel's padding; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the header takes no room from the thread, which has no strip above it and passes under the row; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the tools end short of the window's buttons, and go to their pane's edge where the details panel or a side thread stands beside them; app/src/main/window-frame.test.ts::the row's empty parts move the window, and the strip's own drag cell steps aside over it so that the pill and the buttons press; app/src/main/conversation-header-drag.test.ts::the empty parts of a conversation's header move the window and a double press maximises it once, while its pill and buttons are not taken; app/src/main/conversation-scroll.test.ts::a wheel turned over the header moves the thread under it, or the report open in its place, and leaves a zoom alone; app/scripts/look/checks.mjs::conversation-top-row (scenes top-row-find, top-row-details, top-row-room, top-row-room-find, top-row-scrolled, top-row-wheel, chat, conversation-share, details, raphael-details, room, calm-thread, page-inplace, page-inplace-back, page-inplace-room, page-report, page-report-share, room-thread-open) |

## The accounts of an app, and a sign-in that waits

An app's page listed accounts of sign-ins that were never finished, 「帳號 2 · 等待登入」 and 「帳號 3 · 等待登入」 beside the connected one, and a sign-in cancelled half way could not be removed. And every 連結 button of Connect Apps was grey.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-ACCT-04 | 2026-11-19 | A Composio connection that dropped was never told (the window reads what is stored and nothing read Composio on its own), and signing in again made a second account beside the dead one. Now the core reads the accounts a minute after start and every twenty minutes (only the account list, only while an app is connected, the key is there, nothing holds the lock or waits for a sign-in, and on a Mac only once the key was read in that run by anything, Composio's own calls or the members' tools, so no Keychain question comes unasked; when what works changes, the session's toolkits and the managed server follow, so an app found working again gives the members its tools back; twice as long after a rate limit), and so does every refresh of the list: an account that was connected and has expired, failed, been revoked or gone is told once (`connectors.lost` with the app's id and name, `ConnectionLost` in the window; again only after it worked in between; an app the owner turned off is not told, and a sign-in that never connected is no loss). The window says it in one toast an app, 「{app} 的連線中斷了，重新登入就能繼續使用。」 with 重新登入, which opens that app's page and signs in again. Signing in again takes the place of the accounts that stopped (`connectors.connect` `again: true`): the menu of a dead row has 重新登入 first and names that one (`replace`); 重新登入 on the app (its page or the toast) and a chat's card for an app that has accounts name none, and the core chooses from what it holds, never from the window's list, which may be older than its last read (an app read as needing a sign-in while that list still said connected once made a second account and asked it a name): every account that stopped, and the app's only other account even while Composio still reads it connected (its tools were refused; signing in again to an app with one login is that login again, and it is given back without a revoke). ## Designed for everyone, not for one person

The product is designed for everyone, not for one person. A first-time-user audit (a fresh install: no key, no subscription, no connected app) found words, paths and defaults that only made sense to a long-time user. Only what moves no existing control is changed; the rest is open (the usage page and its limit, the start of the guide, the computer tab's wallpaper, one place for the dictation shortcut, the Composio and Connect Apps flows).

Apps are connected in the sidebar's 「連結應用程式」, a member's messaging is in that member's details and the local-command question is asked on its card, so none of them is written as a Settings path. The Composio website's own 「Settings › Project Settings › API Keys」 is the site's, in its English. The words for Settings are one per language. When a source becomes usable (a provider added or its key replaced, ChatGPT signed in, a subscription program found signed in) the core fills each feature that has no model with that source's recommended one, never one already chosen, two sources at once never overwriting each other, and tells the window once (`models.filled` → `ModelsFilled`, one toast); a subscription program fills only the answer and the quick questions, never text tidying. A feature used with no model, or with a model whose source is off, says which feature in the window's language and never a provider's key (`persona.py`, `models.missing` → `ModelMissing`, at most once a minute), dictation pastes the words as spoken, and the toast's one button opens that feature's choice. The choices list only connected sources (`chooserSources`): a provider turned off or without a key is never listed, even when it holds the choice or a cloud model kept while this computer's speech model is in use; a subscription signed out stays only while it holds the choice, to sign in again there (`holdsCurrent`). A choice whose source is off or signed out stays shown on its own row and card and says 「{source} 未連上」 in the warning colour. The speech models and the catalogue read while Settings is closed are kept and drawn when it opens, so the local list never stays 正在載入. A source found after start is listed at once: ChatGPT's models answer from the list kept at their last reading while it is signed in (`registry._chatgpt_rows`), the Codex drawing check runs beside the providers' readings, and Settings reads the catalogue again at every providers.changed, open or not. ## Where the app's data lives, and what bounds it

The members' computer folders held 7.3 GB in Roaming (`%APPDATA%\Raphael Dev\vm-homes` and `vm-shared`), where only small settings that may follow a profile belong; Claude Code's records of the members' runs had grown to 1,225 folders under the owner's `.claude\projects` in three weeks, 822 of them left by the model listing's temporary folder; and the revision journal of the members' memory documents kept every earlier text for ever. The inventory and the layout are in the feature map (features/member-computer.md, "Where the app's data lives").

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COMP-PLACE-01 | 2026-11-16 | The members' computer folders (`vm-homes`, `vm-shared`) live in the machine-local folder that goes with the data directory (`paths.machine_dir`: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\<the data folder's name>` for a data folder right under `%APPDATA%`; the data folder itself anywhere else, so macOS, the tests and the end-to-end runs keep them where they were). A new install makes them there. An install made before moves them once, at a start (`LocalVm.relocate`, asked at `core.hello`; every preparation of a computer waits for it), and, when that start found a computer of this app running or in use, again as soon as a computer stops while the core runs (`relocate_again` from `stop`: idle, the owner's press or a member's stop; a closing core tries nothing): only while this PC's engine answers and says no computer of this app runs and no carry helper is there, and nothing is being prepared or held; every stopped computer of this app that binds a folder being moved is removed first, so its next start makes it again on the new place with its workspace whole; each folder is renamed on its own volume, never copied, and nothing is deleted. A move that cannot happen (busy, engine silent, another volume, a rename refused) leaves every folder where it is and in use there, and is tried at the next start; a folder that moved is used from its new place even when the other could not follow; a folder of that name made again in the data folder after the move is never used or merged. A target taken before the move is refused (`busy`) instead of making the old place again. Task receipts that name a file in a moved folder are made to name its new place. A session (`<id>.jsonl` and its `<id>` folder) goes when no database of Raphael on this PC records it (`agent_conversation_cli_sessions`, `agent_cli_sessions`, `room_cli_sessions`, this build's and the other build's, read only) and nothing in it changed for seven days; a records folder goes when nothing but an empty `memory` folder is left in it and it is a week old. A member's folders are never touched while a run of that member goes on, and all of them are left when the other build's database cannot be read; a private folder whose run is under way stays; a memory the member wrote and any file it does not know stay. What goes is deleted (the core has no Recycle Bin helper) and the log counts it, never names it.
